Source
Neutral status. The abc conjecture is not proved here. The project neither assumes that inter-universal Teichmüller theory is correct nor that it is wrong; a countermodel to a simplified abstraction is not a counterexample to the papers.

Reference

Every Lean file of the commit: Challenge.lean, Challenge/Defs.lean, Solution.lean and the files under IUT/, grouped by topic. Statements are copied from the source without their proofs; private declarations are omitted. This listing is read from the source text, not from the compiled library; the inventory of the compiled library is data/lean_declarations.json.

Trusted definitions

Challenge/Defs.lean: rad and the abc conjecture in three formulations, on Mathlib only. A reader must check by hand that these definitions say what the abc conjecture says.

Challenge.Defs

Challenge/Defs.lean

Trusted definitions

Module documentation

This file is the reviewed, protected vocabulary of the project. It depends on Mathlib only. Every definition here is meant to be read by a human and compared with its source. The file data/challenge_fingerprints.json records a fingerprint of each declaration, and the trust checks fail when a fingerprint changes without the record being updated on purpose.

Sources: the classical statement of the abc conjecture (Masser, Oesterlé), and Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Theorem A and Corollary 2.3, for the role the statement plays in the four papers.

Public declarations

Challenge.raddef

def rad (n : ℕ) : ℕ := ∏ p ∈ n.primeFactors, p

The radical of a natural number: the product of its distinct prime divisors. rad 0 = 1 and rad 1 = 1, because Nat.primeFactors is empty in both cases.

Challenge.ABCdef

def ABC : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ K : ℝ, 0 < K ∧ ∀ a b c : ℕ, 0 < a → 0 < b → 0 < c →
    Nat.Coprime a b → a + b = c → (c : ℝ) ≤ K * (rad (a * b * c) : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε)

The abc conjecture, constant form. For every real ε > 0 there is a real constant K > 0, depending on ε only, such that every triple of positive integers a, b, c with a and b coprime and a + b = c satisfies c ≤ K * rad (a * b * c) ^ (1 + ε). Because a + b = c, coprimality of a and b is the same as pairwise coprimality of the three numbers; see Solution.coprime_iff_pairwise. The exponent is a real power.

Challenge.ABCFinitedef

def ABCFinite : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → {t : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < t.1 ∧ 0 < t.2.1 ∧ 0 < t.2.2 ∧
    Nat.Coprime t.1 t.2.1 ∧ t.1 + t.2.1 = t.2.2 ∧
    ((rad (t.1 * t.2.1 * t.2.2) : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) < t.2.2)}.Finite

The abc conjecture, finiteness form: for every ε > 0 only finitely many coprime triples a + b = c have c > rad (a * b * c) ^ (1 + ε). This is the primary form in the Formal Conjectures project (FormalConjectures/Wikipedia/ABC.lean, theorem ABC.abc), with the pairwise coprimality written there replaced by the equivalent coprimality of a and b.

Challenge.ABCLogdef

def ABCLog : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ a b c : ℕ, 0 < a → 0 < b → 0 < c →
    Nat.Coprime a b → a + b = c →
      Real.log c ≤ (1 + ε) * Real.log (rad (a * b * c)) + C

The abc conjecture, logarithmic form: log c ≤ (1 + ε) * log (rad (a b c)) + C. This is the shape in which the statement appears as a height inequality (compare the inequality ht ≲ (1 + ε)(log-diff + log-cond) of IUT IV, Theorem A, for the projective line minus three points and rational points).

Challenge statements

Challenge.lean: the seven statements that lake comparator checks, each stated with sorry on purpose. It is not a default target and the axiom gate does not check it. Each statement needs a human comparison with its source.

Challenge

Challenge.lean · imports Challenge.Defs

Challenge: the headline statements

Module documentation

The challenge file of lake comparator (see comparator.json). It states the headline theorems of the project with sorry on purpose; Solution.lean states the same theorems, byte for byte and under the same names, and proves them from the library. The comparator checks that each solution theorem has the statement given here, with the definitions of Challenge.Defs underneath, uses no axiom outside propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound, and is accepted by the kernel.

This file imports only Challenge.Defs, so every statement below is written with Mathlib terms and the reviewed definitions Challenge.rad, Challenge.ABC, Challenge.ABCFinite and Challenge.ABCLog. Definitions of the library that a statement needs are written out in place. No statement here asserts the abc conjecture or its negation; the first two are equivalences between formulations.

This file is not part of any default build target, and nothing imports it. The sorry placeholders are allowed here and nowhere else outside IUT/Experimental/.

No PDF page was read for this file: the statements are taken from the library modules named below, whose docstrings record the pages read. Sources of the statements:

  • the equivalences and the radical: IUT/Applications/ABC.lean;
  • the final inequality of IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, as real arithmetic: IUT/PaperIV/Theorem1_10/Arithmetic.lean, theorem final_inequality, with the definition constant (the constant C_Θ of the statement) written out;
  • the two consequences of Scholze and Stix, Section 1.3, displays (1.4) to (1.6): IUT/Audit/ScholzeStix/Section1_3.lean, with the definitions thetaWithSquares and thetaWithoutSquares written out.
Public declarations

The constant form and the finiteness form of the abc conjecture are equivalent.

theorem abc_iff_abcLog : ABC ↔ ABCLog

The constant form and the logarithmic form of the abc conjecture are equivalent.

Solution.rad_eq_radicaltheorem

theorem rad_eq_radical (n : ℕ) : rad n = UniqueFactorizationMonoid.radical n

The radical of Challenge.Defs is Mathlib's radical on ℕ.

Solution.coprime_iff_pairwisetheorem

theorem coprime_iff_pairwise {a b c : ℕ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) (h : a + b = c) :
  a.Coprime b ↔ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime

For positive a, b with a + b = c, coprimality of a and b is pairwise coprimality of a, b, c.

Solution.theorem1_10_final_arithmetictheorem

theorem theorem1_10_final_arithmetic {l d D E Q q : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d)
  (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hE : 0 ≤ E) (hq : 0 < q)
  (hC : -1 ≤ (l + 1) / (4 * q) * ((1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 10 * E
    - 1 / 6 * (1 - 12 / l ^ 2) * Q) - 1) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, final portion, as real arithmetic: if the constant C_Θ of the statement is at least −1, then (1/6)·log(𝔮) ≤ (1 + 20·d_mod/l)·(log 𝔡 + log 𝔣) + 20·(e*_mod·l + η_prm).

Solution.scholzeStix_with_squarestheorem

theorem scholzeStix_with_squares {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) {l : ℝ} (hl : l = 2 * lh + 1)
  {d qE : ℝ} (h14 : -(qE / (2 * l)) ≤
    (1 / (lh : ℝ)) * ∑ j ∈ Finset.Icc 1 lh, ((j : ℝ) * d - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 * (qE / (2 * l)))) :
  1 / 6 * qE * (1 - 12 / (l * (l + 1))) ≤ d

Scholze and Stix, Section 1.3: substituting the average (1.5), with the factor j², into (1.4) gives (1/6)·qE·(1 − 12/(ℓ(ℓ+1))) ≤ d.

Solution.scholzeStix_without_squarestheorem

theorem scholzeStix_without_squares {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) (l d qE : ℝ) :
  -(qE / (2 * l)) ≤ (1 / (lh : ℝ)) * ∑ j ∈ Finset.Icc 1 lh, ((j : ℝ) * d - qE / (2 * l))
    ↔ 0 ≤ d

Scholze and Stix, Section 1.3: substituting the average (1.6), without the factor j², into (1.4) gives an inequality equivalent to 0 ≤ d.

Foundations

Grothendieck universes on ZFSet and the existence axiom of IUT IV, Section 3. The two existence results take that axiom as an explicit hypothesis.

IUT.Foundations.GrothendieckUniverse

IUT/Foundations/GrothendieckUniverse.lean

Grothendieck universes and the existence axiom of IUT IV, Section 3

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Section 3 (introductory discussion before Definition 3.1), PDF pages 67 and 68 (both rendered and read).

On page 67 the paper recalls that a (Grothendieck) universe V is a set satisfying five axioms (i) to (v), citing [McLn], page 194, and introduces the existence axiom (†G): every set is an element of some universe. A model of ZFC satisfying (†G) is called a ZFCG-model. The paper then derives two consequences of (†G): any set-indexed family of universes is contained, elementwise, in a single larger universe, and in particular there are infinite ascending chains V₀ ∈ V₁ ∈ V₂ ∈ ⋯ ∈ V. On page 68 it notes that, by the axiom of foundation, there are no infinite descending chains V₀ ∋ V₁ ∋ V₂ ∋ ⋯.

This file formalizes the definition and these three remarks over Mathlib's model ZFSet of ZFC set theory. It is not a formalization of the language of species (Definition 3.1 and after), of set-theoretic formulas, or of the "absoluteness" proviso on page 68.

What is and is not proved
  • IsGrothendieckUniverse transcribes axioms (i) to (v) of page 67.
  • The existence axiom (†G) is the proposition ExistenceAxiom. It is not proved: it is not a theorem of ZFC, and nothing here shows that ZFSet.{u} satisfies it. The paper assumes it (page 67) and explicitly leaves aside whether ZFCG-models exist (page 68). The results that use it, exists_universe_forall_mem and exists_ascending_chain, take it as an explicit hypothesis.
  • not_exists_descending_mem_chain is unconditional: it follows from the well-foundedness of ∈ on ZFSet (Mathlib's ZFSet.mem_wf), the formal counterpart of the axiom of foundation. It is proved for arbitrary sets, which covers chains of universes.
  • No Grothendieck universe is exhibited here, so the definition is not shown to be inhabited.
Status of ExistenceAxiom in the model ZFSet.{u}, and the risk of vacuity

ExistenceAxiom.{u} is a statement about the particular model ZFSet.{u}, not about the paper's axiom (†G) in the paper's set theory. Neither it nor its negation is proved here, and we do not expect either to be provable in Lean. The reason is an argument about models of Lean's type theory, which is not formalized: in the standard set-theoretic semantics the universe Type u is interpreted by V_κ for an inaccessible cardinal κ, and ZFSet.{u} then corresponds to V_κ. A Grothendieck universe that is an element of V_κ and contains ω is V_λ for an inaccessible λ < κ.

  • If κ is the least inaccessible cardinal, ZFSet.{u} contains no Grothendieck universe at all, so ExistenceAxiom.{u} is false. In such a model the two conditional theorems exists_universe_forall_mem and exists_ascending_chain are vacuous: their hypothesis fails.
  • If κ is an inaccessible limit of inaccessible cardinals, every element of V_κ lies in some V_λ with λ < κ inaccessible, so ExistenceAxiom.{u} holds. The existence of such a model needs a stronger consistency assumption than the consistency of Lean itself.

So, assuming the consistency of ZFC with enough inaccessible cardinals of the second kind, ExistenceAxiom.{u} is independent of Lean, and the two conditional theorems carry content only in models where it holds. A refutation of ExistenceAxiom was attempted along the lines "an ascending chain gives W containing all V n, and iterating would require unboundedly many universes"; it does not work, because no single set of ZFSet.{u} has rank cofinal in the model, and unboundedly many universes below κ is exactly what the second kind of model provides.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • "The set of natural numbers ℕ" in axiom (ii) is Mathlib's von Neumann ZFSet.omega.
  • In axiom (v), a surjection f : x → y is a set f that is a function from x to y in the sense of Mathlib's ZFSet.IsFunc x y f (a subset of x × y that is single-valued and total on x) and whose image is all of y.
  • In exists_universe_forall_mem, the index set I of the paper is a type I that is u-small, so that the family i ↦ Vᵢ has a set of values (ZFSet.range). This is the formal counterpart of the paper's remark that the graph of the function I ∋ i ↦ Vᵢ is a set. The conclusion holds for any family of sets, not only for a family of universes; the paper's statement is the special case.
Public declarations

IUT.Foundations.IsSurjectiondef

def IsSurjection (x y f : ZFSet.{u}) : Prop :=
  IsFunc x y f ∧ ∀ b ∈ y, ∃ a ∈ x, pair a b ∈ f

A set f is a surjection from x onto y: it is a function from x to y (ZFSet.IsFunc), and every element of y is the value of f at some element of x.

IUT.Foundations.IsGrothendieckUniversestructure

structure IsGrothendieckUniverse (V : ZFSet.{u}) : Prop where

A (Grothendieck) universe, with the five axioms of IUT IV, page 67: (i) V is transitive; (ii) ℕ ∈ V; (iii) V is closed under power sets; (iv) V is closed under unions of its elements; (v) if x ∈ V, y ⊆ V, and f : x → y is a surjection, then y ∈ V.

IUT.Foundations.ExistenceAxiomdef

def ExistenceAxiom : Prop :=
  ∀ x : ZFSet.{u}, ∃ V, IsGrothendieckUniverse V ∧ x ∈ V

The existence axiom (†G) of the Grothendieck school (IUT IV, page 67), read in the model ZFSet.{u}: every set is an element of some universe that is itself an element of ZFSet.{u}. This is an assumption of the paper, not a theorem of ZFC. Neither it nor its negation is proved here; it is false in some models of Lean and true in others (see the module docstring), so theorems that assume it are vacuous in the former.

IUT.Foundations.exists_universe_forall_memtheorem

theorem exists_universe_forall_mem (hG : ExistenceAxiom.{u}) {I : Type*} [Small.{u} I]
  (V : I → ZFSet.{u}) : ∃ W, IsGrothendieckUniverse W ∧ ∀ i, V i ∈ W

IUT IV, page 67: under the existence axiom (†G) (which may fail in ZFSet.{u}; see the module docstring), any family of sets Vᵢ indexed by a set I is contained, elementwise, in a single universe V, that is, Vᵢ ∈ V for all i ∈ I. The paper states this for a family of universes; the proof does not use that the Vᵢ are universes. As in the paper, the proof applies (†G) to the set {Vᵢ} and then uses transitivity (axiom (i)).

IUT.Foundations.exists_ascending_chaintheorem

theorem exists_ascending_chain (hG : ExistenceAxiom.{u}) :
  ∃ V : ℕ → ZFSet.{u}, (∀ n, IsGrothendieckUniverse (V n)) ∧ (∀ n, V n ∈ V (n + 1)) ∧
    ∃ W, IsGrothendieckUniverse W ∧ ∀ n, V n ∈ W

IUT IV, page 67: under the existence axiom (†G) (which may fail in ZFSet.{u}; see the module docstring), there is an infinite ascending chain of universes V₀ ∈ V₁ ∈ V₂ ∈ ⋯ ∈ Vₙ ∈ ⋯ ∈ V, where V is itself a universe.

IUT.Foundations.not_exists_descending_mem_chaintheorem

theorem not_exists_descending_mem_chain :
  ¬ ∃ V : ℕ → ZFSet.{u}, ∀ n, V (n + 1) ∈ V n

IUT IV, page 68: by the axiom of foundation there is no infinite descending chain V₀ ∋ V₁ ∋ V₂ ∋ ⋯. Proved for arbitrary sets, hence in particular for universes, from the well-foundedness of ∈ on ZFSet.

The abc conjecture

Unconditional facts about the formulations of the abc conjecture.

IUT.Applications.ABC

IUT/Applications/ABC.lean · imports Challenge.Defs

The abc conjecture: basic facts and equivalent formulations

Module documentation

Everything here is unconditional. Nothing in this file says anything about whether the abc conjecture holds. The file proves that the three formulations in Challenge.Defs are equivalent, that the radical defined there is the one in Mathlib, and that the coprimality hypothesis can be written in either of the two usual ways.

Public declarations

IUT.ABC.rad_eq_radicaltheorem

theorem rad_eq_radical (n : ℕ) : rad n = UniqueFactorizationMonoid.radical n

The radical of Challenge.Defs is Mathlib's radical on ℕ.

theorem rad_pos (n : ℕ) : 0 < rad n

IUT.ABC.rad_dvdtheorem

theorem rad_dvd (n : ℕ) : rad n ∣ n

theorem rad_le {n : ℕ} (hn : 0 < n) : rad n ≤ n

IUT.ABC.rad_zerotheorem

theorem rad_zero : rad 0 = 1

IUT.ABC.rad_onetheorem

theorem rad_one : rad 1 = 1

IUT.ABC.rad_prime_powtheorem

theorem rad_prime_pow {p k : ℕ} (hp : p.Prime) (hk : k ≠ 0) : rad (p ^ k) = p

IUT.ABC.rad_mul_of_coprimetheorem

theorem rad_mul_of_coprime {a b : ℕ} (h : a.Coprime b) : rad (a * b) = rad a * rad b

IUT.ABC.one_le_rad_casttheorem

theorem one_le_rad_cast (n : ℕ) : (1 : ℝ) ≤ rad n

IUT.ABC.one_le_rad_rpowtheorem

theorem one_le_rad_rpow (n : ℕ) {y : ℝ} (hy : 0 ≤ y) : (1 : ℝ) ≤ (rad n : ℝ) ^ y

IUT.ABC.coprime_iff_pairwisetheorem

theorem coprime_iff_pairwise {a b c : ℕ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) (h : a + b = c) :
  a.Coprime b ↔ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime

For positive a, b with a + b = c, coprimality of a and b is the same as pairwise coprimality of a, b, c in the form used by the Formal Conjectures project. Positivity is needed: for a = b = c = 0 the set {a, b, c} is a singleton, so it is pairwise coprime, while 0 and 0 are not coprime.

IUT.ABC.abcLog_of_abctheorem

theorem abcLog_of_abc (h : ABC) : ABCLog

The constant form implies the logarithmic form, with C = log K.

IUT.ABC.abc_of_abcLogtheorem

theorem abc_of_abcLog (h : ABCLog) : ABC

The logarithmic form implies the constant form, with K = exp C.

IUT.ABC.abc_iff_abcLogtheorem

theorem abc_iff_abcLog : ABC ↔ ABCLog

IUT.ABC.abc_of_abcFinitetheorem

theorem abc_of_abcFinite (h : ABCFinite) : ABC

The finiteness form implies the constant form. The constant is one plus a bound for the values of c over the finitely many exceptional triples.

IUT.ABC.abcFinite_of_abctheorem

theorem abcFinite_of_abc (h : ABC) : ABCFinite

The constant form implies the finiteness form. One applies the constant form with ε / 2: an exceptional triple for ε then has rad ^ (ε / 2) < K, which bounds the radical, hence c, hence a and b.

IUT.ABC.abc_iff_abcFinitetheorem

theorem abc_iff_abcFinite : ABC ↔ ABCFinite

IUT.ABC.hypotheses_nonvacuoustheorem

theorem hypotheses_nonvacuous :
  ∃ a b c : ℕ, 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ Nat.Coprime a b ∧ a + b = c

The set of triples the conjecture quantifies over is not empty: 1 + 1 = 2. So no formulation above is true for the empty reason that nothing satisfies its hypotheses.

IUT.Applications.ABCFormalConjectures

IUT/Applications/ABCFormalConjectures.lean · imports IUT.Applications.ABC

Comparison with the abc statements of the Formal Conjectures project

Module documentation

Source: the file FormalConjectures/Wikipedia/ABC.lean of https://github.com/google-deepmind/formal-conjectures, at commit 9097e2f90e66d2f6244209cb1c07f7120d17ec53 (2026-11-24). The file is byte-identical to the version at commit 1646ca16afd6cc7a693d3bdc9f066c4d3cc01a89, the commit cited by the lana-agents/iut repository. The project is licensed under Apache-2.0 (Copyright 2025 The Formal Conjectures Authors).

That file cannot be imported here (it is a Lean module of a different project, on Lean v4.33.1, and its theorems are stated with sorry). This file restates, in the namespace IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures, their definitions ABC.radical and ABC.quality with the same bodies, and turns each of their three open theorems into a definition of type Prop whose body is the theorem statement with the arguments (ε : ℝ) (hε : 0 < ε) written as ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →; binders, set-builders and coercions are as in their file:

  • abc (their ABC.abc): finiteness of the exceptional triples, with pairwise coprimality of the set {a, b, c};
  • ltConstantMul (their ABC.abc.variants.lt_constant_mul): a real constant K, with no sign condition, and the strict inequality c < K * rad(abc)^(1 + ε);
  • qualityVariant (their ABC.abc.variants.quality): finiteness of the triples of quality q(a, b, c) = log c / log rad(abc) greater than 1 + ε.

Each is proved equivalent to Challenge.ABC. Nothing here says whether any of them holds.

Remarks on the formulations, all checked by the proofs below.

  • Coprimality: for positive a, b with a + b = c, pairwise coprimality of {a, b, c} is coprimality of a and b (IUT.ABC.coprime_iff_pairwise).
  • Constant form: Challenge.ABC asks for 0 < K and the non-strict ≤; theirs asks for no sign and the strict <. These are equivalent: the triple 1 + 1 = 2 forces K > 0 in their form, and K + 1 turns ≤ into < because rad(abc)^(1 + ε) ≥ 1.
  • Quality form: Lean's division gives x / 0 = 0, so the quality is 0 when rad(abc) = 1. For positive a, b with a + b = c this never happens: c ≥ 2, so rad(abc) ≥ 2 (rad(abc) = 1 with abc ≠ 0 forces abc = 1, hence a = b = c = 1, and 1 + 1 ≠ 1). When rad(abc) ≥ 2 the logarithm of the radical is positive, and q > 1 + ε is exactly rad(abc)^(1 + ε) < c. So, for each ε, the quality set is the same set as the set of the finiteness form (qualitySet_eq), and the quality form is exactly equivalent; no strict versus non-strict discrepancy arises, since both sets use strict inequalities.

No PDF page of the IUT papers is used in this file.

Public declarations

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.radicaldef

def radical (n : ℕ) : ℕ := n.primeFactors.prod id

The radical of n denoted is the product of the distinct prime factors of n. (Their ABC.radical, same body.)

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.qualitydef

noncomputable def quality (a b c : ℕ) : ℝ := (c : ℝ).log / (radical <| a * b * c : ℝ).log

Quality q(a, b, c) of the triple (a, b, c) is defined as q(a,b,c) = log (c) / log (rad(abc)). (Their ABC.quality, same body.)

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.abcdef

def abc : Prop := ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
  {(a, b, c) : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧
  a + b = c ∧ (radical <| a * b * c : ℝ)^(1 + ε) < c}.Finite

For every positive real number ε, there exist only finitely many triples (a, b, c) of coprime positive integers, with a + b = c, such that c > rad(abc)^(1+ε). (Statement of their theorem ABC.abc.)

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.ltConstantMuldef

def ltConstantMul : Prop := ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ K,
  ∀ (a b c : ℕ), 0 < a → 0 < b → 0 < c → ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime →
  a + b = c → c < K * (radical <| a * b * c : ℝ)^(1 + ε)

For every positive real number ε, there exists a constant K_ε such that for all triples (a, b, c) of coprime positive integers, with a + b = c we have c < K_ε rad(abc)^(1+ε). (Statement of their theorem ABC.abc.variants.lt_constant_mul.)

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.qualityVariantdef

def qualityVariant : Prop := ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
  {(a, b, c) : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧
  a + b = c ∧ quality a b c > (1 + ε)}.Finite

For every positive real number ε, there exist only finitely many triples (a, b, c) of coprime positive integers with a + b = c such that q(a, b, c) > 1 + ε. (Statement of their theorem ABC.abc.variants.quality.)

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.two_le_radtheorem

theorem two_le_rad {n : ℕ} (hn : 2 ≤ n) : 2 ≤ rad n

rad n ≥ 2 for n ≥ 2: some prime divides n, hence divides rad n.

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.quality_gt_ifftheorem

theorem quality_gt_iff {a b c : ℕ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) (h : a + b = c) (ε : ℝ) :
  quality a b c > 1 + ε ↔ (rad (a * b * c) : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) < c

For positive a, b with a + b = c, the quality exceeds 1 + ε exactly when rad(abc)^(1 + ε) < c.

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.abcSet_eqtheorem

theorem abcSet_eq (ε : ℝ) :
  {(a, b, c) : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧
    a + b = c ∧ (radical <| a * b * c : ℝ)^(1 + ε) < c} =
  {t : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < t.1 ∧ 0 < t.2.1 ∧ 0 < t.2.2 ∧
    Nat.Coprime t.1 t.2.1 ∧ t.1 + t.2.1 = t.2.2 ∧
    ((rad (t.1 * t.2.1 * t.2.2) : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) < t.2.2)}

The set of the finiteness form of Formal Conjectures is the set of Challenge.ABCFinite.

IUT.ABC.FormalConjectures.qualitySet_eqtheorem

theorem qualitySet_eq (ε : ℝ) :
  {(a, b, c) : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧
    a + b = c ∧ quality a b c > (1 + ε)} =
  {(a, b, c) : ℕ × ℕ × ℕ | 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < c ∧ ({a, b, c} : Set ℕ).Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧
    a + b = c ∧ (radical <| a * b * c : ℝ)^(1 + ε) < c}

The set of the quality form is the set of the finiteness form, for every ε.

Prerequisites

General mathematics needed by the papers.

IUT.Prerequisites.PositiveRationalsZhatUnits

IUT/Prerequisites/PositiveRationalsZhatUnits.lean

The elementary fact ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Example 5.1 (v), PDF page 127 (rendered and read), where the injectivity of a Kummer map is deduced "from the elementary observation that, relative to the natural inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ, ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}". The same fact is invoked in Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 2.3.3 (vi), (vii), (viii), PDF pages 81 to 83 (text only, from the extracted text), and in Remark 3.12.1 (iii), PDF pages 186 and 187 (rendered and read), where it is described as playing a key role in the multiradial algorithms for cyclotomic rigidity isomorphisms in the number field case, and as an immediate consequence of an interpretation of the product formula.

This file is not a formalization of Example 5.1; it proves only the elementary arithmetic fact quoted above.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • Mathlib has no Ẑ. We model Ẑ as the product ∏_p ℤ_p, written Zhat, indexed by the nonzero primes of ℤ (IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum ℤ), with ℤ_p the ring v.adicCompletionIntegers ℚ of Mathlib.
  • Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is modeled by Mathlib's finite adele ring IsDedekindDomain.FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ, the restricted product of the ℚ_p with respect to the ℤ_p. The isomorphism Ẑ ⊗ ℚ ≅ 𝔸_f is standard but is not proved here. The inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is Mathlib's algebraMap ℚ (FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ), and Ẑ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is the ring map Zhat.toFiniteAdeles defined below (the restricted product contains the full product of the integer rings).
  • ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× is read as the set of positive rationals whose image in 𝔸_f is the image of a unit of Ẑ. The main theorem posRat_inter_zhatUnits says this set is {1}.

The core of the proof is eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_one: a positive rational number that is a unit at every prime is 1. It uses Mathlib's IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum.mem_integers_of_valuation_le_one.

Public declarations

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhatabbrev

abbrev Zhat : Type := Π v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ, v.adicCompletionIntegers ℚ

Ẑ, modeled as the product ∏_p ℤ_p of the rings of p-adic integers over the nonzero primes of ℤ.

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhat.toFiniteAdelesdef

noncomputable def Zhat.toFiniteAdeles : Zhat →+* FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ where
  toFun x := ⟨fun v => (x v : v.adicCompletion ℚ), Filter.Eventually.of_forall fun v => (x v).2⟩
  map_one' := rfl
  map_mul' _ _ := rfl
  map_zero' := rfl
  map_add' _ _ := rfl

The natural ring map Ẑ → Ẑ ⊗ ℚ, with Ẑ ⊗ ℚ modeled by the finite adeles of ℚ: an element of ∏_p ℤ_p is integral at every prime, so it lies in the restricted product.

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhat.toFiniteAdeles_applytheorem

@[simp]
theorem Zhat.toFiniteAdeles_apply (x : Zhat) (v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ) :
    Zhat.toFiniteAdeles x v = (x v : v.adicCompletion ℚ)

IUT.Prerequisites.eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_onetheorem

theorem eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_one {q : ℚ} (hq : 0 < q)
  (h : ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ, v.valuation ℚ q = 1) : q = 1

A positive rational number whose v-adic valuation is 1 at every nonzero prime v of ℤ is equal to 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.posRat_inter_zhatUnitstheorem

theorem posRat_inter_zhatUnits :
  {q : ℚ | 0 < q ∧ ∃ u : Zhatˣ,
    Zhat.toFiniteAdeles (u : Zhat) = algebraMap ℚ (FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ) q} = {1}

**ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}** (IUT I, page 127), relative to the natural inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ = 𝔸_f: the positive rationals whose image in the finite adeles is the image of a unit of Ẑ = ∏_p ℤ_p are exactly {1}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ProductFormula

IUT/Prerequisites/ProductFormula.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

Normalized absolute values and the product formula over 𝕍(F)

Module documentation

Sources:

  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, PDF pages 21 and 22 (rendered and read): the set 𝕍(F) of valuations of a number field F, as the disjoint union of the nonarchimedean and archimedean valuations, formalized in IUT/PaperIV/ArithmeticDivisor.lean as IUT.PaperIV.Valuations F.
  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 3.12.1 (iii), PDF pages 186 and 187 (rendered and read), which describes the fact ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1} as an immediate consequence of an interpretation of the product formula.

This file is not a formalization of any numbered statement of the papers. It transports the product formula of Mathlib (NumberField.prod_abs_eq_one, in Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/ProductFormula.lean) to the index set IUT.PaperIV.Valuations F used for arithmetic divisors in this project, in multiplicative and in additive (logarithmic) form.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • The normalized absolute value ‖x‖_v at a nonarchimedean v is Mathlib's NumberField.FinitePlace.mk v x, which equals q_v ^ (- ord_v x) with q_v the cardinality of the residue field (see the module docstring of the Mathlib file above).
  • At an archimedean v it is |x|_v ^ m_v, with m_v = 1 for a real place and m_v = 2 for a complex place (NumberField.InfinitePlace.mult). The papers do not fix this normalization on the pages read; it is the one for which the product formula holds with all multiplicities one. Note that the degree map of IUT IV Definition 1.9 (i) gives weight 1 to every archimedean valuation, real or complex; no relation between the two conventions is asserted here.
Public declarations
variable {F : Type*} [Field F] [NumberField F]

IUT.Prerequisites.finprod_sum_typetheorem

theorem finprod_sum_type {α β M : Type*} [CommMonoid M] (f : α ⊕ β → M)
  (hf : (Function.mulSupport f).Finite) :
  ∏ᶠ i, f i = (∏ᶠ a, f (.inl a)) * ∏ᶠ b, f (.inr b)

A finite product over a sum type splits into the two partial products.

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbsdef

noncomputable def normAbs : Valuations F → F → ℝ
  | .inl v, x => FinitePlace.mk v x
  | .inr w, x => w x ^ w.mult

The normalized absolute value ‖x‖_v at a valuation v ∈ 𝕍(F): Mathlib's finite place absolute value (q_v ^ (- ord_v x)) at a nonarchimedean v, and |x|_v ^ m_v at an archimedean v, where m_v ∈ {1, 2} is 1 for a real place and 2 for a complex place.

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_nonnegtheorem

theorem normAbs_nonneg (v : Valuations F) (x : F) : 0 ≤ normAbs v x

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_postheorem

theorem normAbs_pos (v : Valuations F) {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) : 0 < normAbs v x

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_mulSupport_finitetheorem

theorem normAbs_mulSupport_finite {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) :
  (Function.mulSupport fun v : Valuations F => normAbs v x).Finite

For x ≠ 0, ‖x‖_v = 1 for all but finitely many v ∈ 𝕍(F).

IUT.Prerequisites.prod_normAbs_eq_onetheorem

theorem prod_normAbs_eq_one {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) : ∏ᶠ v : Valuations F, normAbs v x = 1

**Product formula over 𝕍(F)**: for x ∈ F nonzero, ∏_{v ∈ 𝕍(F)} ‖x‖_v = 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.sum_log_normAbs_eq_zerotheorem

theorem sum_log_normAbs_eq_zero {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) :
  ∑ᶠ v : Valuations F, Real.log (normAbs v x) = 0

**Product formula over 𝕍(F), additive form**: for x ∈ F nonzero, ∑_{v ∈ 𝕍(F)} log ‖x‖_v = 0.

IUT I

Construction of Hodge theaters.

IUT.PaperI.InitialThetaData

IUT/PaperI/InitialThetaData.lean

IUT I, Definition 3.1: the conditions on initial Θ-data that Mathlib can state today

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Definition 3.1, pages 61 to 63, with Remarks 3.1.1 to 3.1.7 (pages 63 to 69, text only) for context. Pages 61, 62, 63 were read as rendered images; every condition transcribed below was checked against those images.

This file is not a formalization of Definition 3.1. Initial Θ-data is a tuple (F̄/F, X_F, l, C_K, V, V^bad_mod, ε) subject to conditions (a) to (f). Most of the objects involved (hyperbolic orbicurves of type (1, l-tors)±, K-cores, étale fundamental groups of orbicurves, the field of moduli of X_F in the sense of [AbsTopIII], Definition 5.1, (ii), the q-parameters of a Tate curve, cusps arising from the quotient Q of [EtTh], Definition 2.1) have no counterpart in Mathlib. This file states, as separate named predicates on an actual number field F and an actual Weierstrass curve W over F, exactly those clauses whose every term exists in Mathlib with its intended meaning, and proves a few small facts.

What is stated here
  • SqrtNegOneMem F: the clause √−1 ∈ F of condition (a).
  • AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause l: only the first clause "l is a prime number ≥ 5" of condition (c), not the whole condition.
  • TorsionRational W n: every n-torsion point of E_F over an algebraic closure is F-rational. With n = 6 this is the clause "the 2 · 3-torsion points of E_F are rational over F" of condition (b).
  • SemistableAtFinitePlaces W: the clause "X_F admits stable reduction over all v ∈ 𝕍(F)^non" of condition (b), read as described below.
  • Relative to a number field k with F an algebra over k, standing in for F_mod: BadSetOddNonempty, MultiplicativeOverBadSet, GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo, PrimeToBadResidueChars. These state the clauses of (b) and (c) that mention F_mod and 𝕍^bad_mod, for an arbitrary such k; the requirement that k be the field of moduli of X_F is not stated, because the field of moduli is not defined here.
  • PartialInitialThetaConditions: the conjunction of the predicates above. It is a partial collection, not Definition 3.1; its docstring lists what is missing.
Interpretation choices, recorded
  • F̄ is taken to be Mathlib's AlgebraicClosure F. The rationality of torsion points does not depend on the choice of algebraic closure; that independence is not proved here.
  • E_F is represented by a Weierstrass curve W with W.IsElliptic. Points of E_F over a field L are (W⁄L).toAffine.Point (the point at infinity together with the affine nonsingular points), with Mathlib's group law.
  • Stable reduction of the once-punctured elliptic curve X_F at a finite place is read as: a minimal Weierstrass model of E_F over the valuation ring of F at that place has good or multiplicative reduction. This identification of "stable reduction of X_F" with "semistable reduction of E_F" is the standard one but is not argued on the pages read; it is a reading of page 61, not a transcription. Mathlib's WeierstrassCurve.minimal is a chosen minimal model; the reduction type does not depend on the choice, but that is not proved here.
  • "Bad (i.e., multiplicative) reduction" at the places over 𝕍^bad_mod is page 61's own gloss and is stated with HasMultiplicativeReduction.
  • "Odd residue characteristic" at a prime w of 𝓞 k is stated as (2 : 𝓞 k) ∉ w, and "l prime to the residue characteristic of w" as (l : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.
  • A place of F lies over a place w of k when the prime ideals satisfy v ∩ 𝓞 k = w.
Not statable today (see docs/INITIAL_THETA_DATA.md)

The field of moduli F_mod of X_F; the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) containing SL₂(𝔽_l) and the field K it cuts out (Mathlib has the group law on E(F̄) and the Galois action can be written with WeierstrassCurve.Affine.Point.map, but the structure theorem E[l](F̄) ≅ (ℤ/lℤ)² that gives the representation its meaning is not in Mathlib); the orders of the q-parameters at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; the orbicurves C_F, C_K, X_K and their types; K-cores; the étale and tempered fundamental groups Π, Δ; the section V ⊆ 𝕍(K) (statable once K is constructed, omitted here); the cusp ε and the condition at v ∈ V^bad that C_v be of type (1, ℤ/lℤ)±.

No claim of the form l ≠ 5 is made: it would need the mod l Galois representation.

IUT.PaperI.SqrtNegOneMemdef

def SqrtNegOneMem (F : Type*) [Field F] : Prop :=
  ∃ x : F, x ^ 2 = -1

Definition 3.1 (a), first clause, page 61: the number field F contains a square root of −1.

IUT.PaperI.not_sqrtNegOneMem_rattheorem

theorem not_sqrtNegOneMem_rat : ¬ SqrtNegOneMem ℚ

The clause of condition (a) is not automatic: it fails for ℚ.

IUT.PaperI.AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClausedef

def AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause (l : ℕ) : Prop :=
  l.Prime ∧ 5 ≤ l

Definition 3.1 (c), first clause only, page 62: l is a prime number with l ≥ 5. Condition (c) also requires that the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) contain SL₂(𝔽_l) and that l be prime to the residue characteristics of 𝕍^bad_mod and to the orders of the q-parameters at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; this predicate does not state those clauses.

IUT.PaperI.auxiliaryPrimeFirstClause_fivetheorem

theorem auxiliaryPrimeFirstClause_five : AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause 5

The clause of condition (c) is satisfiable: 5 is a prime ≥ 5.

IUT.PaperI.AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause.coprime_sixtheorem

theorem AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause.coprime_six {l : ℕ} (h : AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause l) : Nat.Coprime l 6

A prime l ≥ 5 is prime to 6, as the hypotheses of §1 (page 37) require of l.

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRationaldef

def TorsionRational (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ P : (W⁄(AlgebraicClosure F)).toAffine.Point, n • P = 0 →
    P ∈ (Affine.Point.baseChange (W' := W) F (AlgebraicClosure F)).range

Every n-torsion point of E_F over F̄ (here AlgebraicClosure F) is F-rational, that is, lies in the image of the base change map E(F) → E(F̄). With n = 6 this is the clause of Definition 3.1 (b), page 62: "the 2 · 3-torsion points of E_F are rational over F".

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRational.of_dvdtheorem

theorem TorsionRational.of_dvd {m n : ℕ} (hmn : m ∣ n) (h : TorsionRational W n) :
  TorsionRational W m

Rationality of the n-torsion implies rationality of the m-torsion for m ∣ n.

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRational.two_of_sixtheorem

theorem TorsionRational.two_of_six (h : TorsionRational W 6) : TorsionRational W 2

The 2-torsion part, used on page 63 (Definition 3.1 (e)) and page 71 (Example 3.2 (iv)): rationality of the 6-torsion gives rationality of the 2-torsion.

IUT.PaperI.SemistableAtFinitePlacesdef

def SemistableAtFinitePlaces : Prop :=
  ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F),
    (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasGoodReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring ∨
      (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasMultiplicativeReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61: X_F admits stable reduction at every nonarchimedean valuation of F, read as: at every nonzero prime v of 𝓞 F, a minimal model of E_F over the valuation ring of v has good or multiplicative reduction. See the module docstring for this interpretation.

IUT.PaperI.BadSetOddNonemptydef

def BadSetOddNonempty (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop :=
  S.Nonempty ∧ ∀ w ∈ S, (2 : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.asIdeal

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61, relative to a number field k standing in for F_mod: S (standing in for 𝕍^bad_mod) is a nonempty set of nonarchimedean valuations of k of odd residue characteristic.

IUT.PaperI.MultiplicativeOverBadSetdef

def MultiplicativeOverBadSet (W : WeierstrassCurve F) (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) :
    Prop :=
  ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F), ∀ w ∈ S,
    v.asIdeal.comap (algebraMap (𝓞 k) (𝓞 F)) = w.asIdeal →
      (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasMultiplicativeReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61, relative to k: E_F has bad, that is multiplicative, reduction at every nonarchimedean valuation of F lying over an element of S.

IUT.PaperI.GaloisOfDegreePrimeTodef

def GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo (l : ℕ) : Prop :=
  IsGalois k F ∧ Nat.Coprime (Module.finrank k F) l

Definition 3.1 (b), pages 61 and 62, relative to k: the extension F/k is Galois of degree prime to l.

IUT.PaperI.PrimeToBadResidueCharsdef

def PrimeToBadResidueChars (l : ℕ) (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop :=
  ∀ w ∈ S, (l : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.asIdeal

Definition 3.1 (c), page 62, relative to k: l is prime to the residue characteristics of the elements of S.

IUT.PaperI.PartialInitialThetaConditionsstructure

structure PartialInitialThetaConditions (W : WeierstrassCurve F) [W.IsElliptic] (l : ℕ)
  (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop where

A partial collection of the conditions of Definition 3.1 (pages 61 to 63): the clauses that are statable with Mathlib today, for a number field F, an elliptic curve W standing for E_F, a natural number l, a number field k with F a k-algebra standing in for F_mod, and a set S standing in for 𝕍^bad_mod. This is not initial Θ-data. Missing, relative to Definition 3.1: * (a) nothing beyond the choice F̄ = AlgebraicClosure F; * (b) that X_F is the once-punctured curve E_F \ {0} (implicit in using W), the orbicurve C_F, the requirement that k be the field of moduli F_mod of X_F, the field F_sol, and the fundamental groups Π_{X_F} ⊆ Π_{C_F}, Δ_X ⊆ Δ_C; * (c) that the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) contain SL₂(𝔽_l), the field K, and that l be prime to the orders of the q-parameters of E_F at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; * (d) the orbicurve C_K of type (1, l-tors)± with K-core C_F ×_F K, and X_K; * (e) the section V ⊆ 𝕍(K) of 𝕍(K) → 𝕍_mod and the type (1, ℤ/lℤ)± condition at v ∈ V^bad; * (f) the cusp ε and its normalization at v ∈ V^bad. Open obligation: no inhabitant is exhibited. This file does not construct any (F, W, l, k, S) satisfying these conditions, so it does not show that the conjunction is consistent. A witness needs at least: a number field F containing a primitive fourth root of unity and the whole 6-torsion of E_F (hence, by the Weil pairing, also a primitive cube root of unity, so F ⊇ ℚ(ζ₁₂); for example F = k(E[6], √−1) for a curve over a number field k); semistable reduction of E_F at every finite place, checked on minimal models; a nonempty set S of odd places of k at which E_F has multiplicative reduction above S; F/k Galois of degree prime to l; and a prime l ≥ 5 prime to the residue characteristics in S. None of these has been verified for a specific curve.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism

IUT/PaperI/PolyMorphism.lean

IUT I, Section 0: poly-morphisms and capsules

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Section 0 ("Notations and Conventions"), subsection "Monoids and Categories", pages 33 and 34. Both pages were read as rendered images for this file; every definition below was checked against those images.

These are the unnumbered definitions of poly-morphisms and capsules that the later sections of IUT I to IV use throughout (for example, the radial data of IUT II, Example 1.8 (i), page 35, read as a rendered image, include a full poly-isomorphism of topological groups Π/Δ ⥲ G). They are elementary and are stated here in full, for an arbitrary category C of Mathlib.

Definitions of page 33, stated in full
  • A poly-morphism A → B is a collection of morphisms A → B, that is, a subset of the set of morphisms A → B (PolyMorphism). The page says "a subset"; the empty subset is allowed and no nonemptiness is imposed.
  • It is a poly-isomorphism if all of its members are isomorphisms (IsPolyIso), and a poly-automorphism if moreover A = B (IsPolyAut).
  • The full poly-isomorphism A ⥲ B is the poly-morphism consisting of all isomorphisms A ⥲ B (fullPolyIso).
  • The composite of {fᵢ : A → B}_{i ∈ I} with {g_j : B → C}_{j ∈ J} is the set {g_j ∘ fᵢ}_{(i,j) ∈ I × J}, multiplicities ignored (PolyMorphism.comp). Mathlib writes composition in diagrammatic order, so g_j ∘ fᵢ is fᵢ ≫ g_j. Using sets realizes "the set [where multiplicities are ignored]".
Definitions of pages 33 and 34
  • A capsule of objects of C is a finite collection {A_j}_{j ∈ J} of objects indexed by a finite index set J; it is a |J|-capsule, and π₀({A_j}) = J (Capsule). The index set is a type with a Fintype instance.
  • A morphism of capsules {A_j}_{j ∈ J} → {A'_{j'}}_{j' ∈ J'} is an injection ι : J ↪ J' together with a morphism A_j → A'_{ι(j)} for each j (Capsule.Hom). Composition and identities are given (Capsule.Hom.comp, Capsule.Hom.id). Not done here: the page states that capsules form a category Capsule(C); the category laws are not proved in this file, so no Category instance is given.
  • A capsule-full poly-morphism associated to a fixed injection ι : J ↪ J' is the set of capsule morphisms with that injection whose components are isomorphisms A_j ⥲ A'_{ι(j)} (capsuleFullPolyMorphism); it is a capsule-full poly-isomorphism when ι is a bijection. IsCapsuleFullPolyIso states only this bijectivity condition on ι, not the whole notion.

Nothing in this file is a theorem of the papers; the lemmas only record elementary properties of the definitions.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphismabbrev

abbrev PolyMorphism (A B : C) : Type v := Set (A ⟶ B)

IUT I, page 33: a poly-morphism A → B is a collection of morphisms A → B, that is, a subset of the set of morphisms A → B.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyIsodef

def IsPolyIso {A B : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) : Prop :=
  ∀ f ∈ S, IsIso f

IUT I, page 33: a poly-morphism is a poly-isomorphism if all of the morphisms in the collection are isomorphisms.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyAutdef

def IsPolyAut {A : C} (S : PolyMorphism A A) : Prop :=
  IsPolyIso S

IUT I, page 33: a poly-isomorphism A ⥲ A (with A = B) is a poly-automorphism.

IUT.PaperI.fullPolyIsodef

def fullPolyIso (A B : C) : PolyMorphism A B :=
  {f | IsIso f}

IUT I, page 33: the full poly-isomorphism A ⥲ B is the poly-morphism given by the collection of all isomorphisms A ⥲ B.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism.compdef

def PolyMorphism.comp {A B D : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) (T : PolyMorphism B D) :
    PolyMorphism A D :=
  Set.image2 (fun f g => f ≫ g) S T

IUT I, page 33: the composite of a poly-morphism {fᵢ : A → B} with a poly-morphism {g_j : B → C} is the set {g_j ∘ fᵢ} (multiplicities ignored). In Mathlib's diagrammatic notation g_j ∘ fᵢ is fᵢ ≫ g_j.

IUT.PaperI.isPolyIso_fullPolyIsotheorem

theorem isPolyIso_fullPolyIso (A B : C) : IsPolyIso (fullPolyIso A B)

The full poly-isomorphism is a poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.isPolyIso_iff_subsettheorem

theorem isPolyIso_iff_subset {A B : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) :
  IsPolyIso S ↔ S ⊆ fullPolyIso A B

A poly-morphism is a poly-isomorphism if and only if it is contained in the full poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyIso.comptheorem

theorem IsPolyIso.comp {A B D : C} {S : PolyMorphism A B} {T : PolyMorphism B D}
  (hS : IsPolyIso S) (hT : IsPolyIso T) : IsPolyIso (S.comp T)

The composite of two poly-isomorphisms is a poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism.comp_assoctheorem

theorem PolyMorphism.comp_assoc {A B D E : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) (T : PolyMorphism B D)
  (U : PolyMorphism D E) : (S.comp T).comp U = S.comp (T.comp U)

Composition of poly-morphisms is associative.

IUT.PaperI.Capsulestructure

structure Capsule where

IUT I, pages 33 and 34: a capsule of objects of C is a finite collection {A_j}_{j ∈ J} of objects of C indexed by a finite index set J.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.π₀abbrev

abbrev π₀ (A : Capsule C) : Type := A.index

IUT I, page 34: π₀({A_j}_{j ∈ J}) = J.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.IsNCapsuledef

def IsNCapsule (A : Capsule C) (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  Fintype.card A.index = n

IUT I, page 34: a capsule with index set J is a |J|-capsule.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Homstructure

structure Hom (A A' : Capsule C) where

IUT I, page 34: a morphism of capsules {A_j}_{j ∈ J} → {A'_{j'}}_{j' ∈ J'} consists of an injection ι : J ↪ J' and, for each j ∈ J, a morphism A_j → A'_{ι(j)}.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Hom.iddef

def Hom.id (A : Capsule C) : Hom A A :=
  ⟨Function.Embedding.refl _, fun j => 𝟙 (A.obj j)⟩

The identity morphism of a capsule.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Hom.compdef

def Hom.comp {A A' A'' : Capsule C} (φ : Hom A A') (ψ : Hom A' A'') : Hom A A'' :=
  ⟨φ.ι.trans ψ.ι, fun j => φ.app j ≫ ψ.app (φ.ι j)⟩

Composition of morphisms of capsules: compose the injections and the component morphisms.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.capsuleFullPolyMorphismdef

def capsuleFullPolyMorphism (A A' : Capsule C) (ι : A.index ↪ A'.index) : Set (Hom A A') :=
  {φ | φ.ι = ι ∧ ∀ j, IsIso (φ.app j)}

IUT I, page 34: the capsule-full poly-morphism {A_j} → {A'_{j'}} associated to a fixed injection ι : J ↪ J' is the set of capsule morphisms with injection ι whose components A_j → A'_{ι(j)} are isomorphisms.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.IsCapsuleFullPolyIsodef

def IsCapsuleFullPolyIso {A A' : Capsule C} (ι : A.index ↪ A'.index) : Prop :=
  Function.Bijective ι

IUT I, page 34: a capsule-full poly-morphism is a capsule-full poly-isomorphism when the associated injection between index sets is a bijection.

IUT II

Hodge–Arakelov-theoretic evaluation.

IUT.PaperII.Labels

IUT/PaperII/Labels.lean

IUT II: the label sets |𝔽_l|, 𝔽_l^⋇ and the exponents j² of the theta values

Module documentation

Sources, all read for this file, with every formula below checked against a rendered page:

  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, page 3 (rendered): the definitions l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 and 𝔽_l^⋇ = 𝔽_l^× / {±1}.
  • Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory II, page 3 (rendered): j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇} is uniquely determined by its image in |𝔽_l| = 𝔽_l / {±1} = {0} ∪ 𝔽_l^⋇.
  • IUT II, Remark 2.5.1 (i), page 72 (rendered): the theta value attached to j ∈ |𝔽_l| is qq_v^{j²} with j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇} the representative of j. Here qq_v (a q with a double underline in the paper) is not the Tate parameter q_v of E_v: by IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv), page 71 (rendered), qq_v = q_v^{1/2l}, a chosen 2l-th root determined up to a μ_{2l}-multiple. Relative to the Tate parameter the theta value is q_v^{j²/(2l)}. The exponents treated in this file are the j² attached to qq_v.
  • IUT II, Proposition 4.1 (iv), page 122, and Remark 4.5.4, page 136 (both rendered): the Gaussian monoid carries the exponent vector (…, j² · log(p_v), …) indexed by j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇, with j read as a natural number in {1, …, l^⋇}.
  • IUT II, Remark 4.7.3 (iii), page 145 (rendered): the volume at each j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ gets weight 1/l^⋇.
  • IUT II, Remark 2.6.3 (ii), pages 79 and 80 (rendered): the quantity ‖Γ′‖ = |Γ′|⁻¹ · ∑_{j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{j ∈ j ∩ Γ′} j² and claim (d) about it.
What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of any theorem about theta functions or Gaussian monoids. It checks elementary facts about the labels and the exponents that these constructions use:

  1. |𝔽_l| (defined as the quotient of ZMod l by x ∼ ±x) is in bijection with {0, 1, …, l^⋇} for odd l (page 3 of IUT II), so it has l^⋇ + 1 elements and l^⋇ nonzero elements.
  2. 𝔽_l^⋇ = 𝔽_l^× / {±1} (IUT I, page 3) has l^⋇ elements for an odd prime l.
  3. 24 · ∑_{j=1}^{l^⋇} j² = l (l² − 1) and the 1/l^⋇-weighted average of the exponents j² equals l (l + 1) / 12. Neither formula appears in the pages read; they are elementary arithmetic about the exponent vector of pages 122, 136, 145.
  4. Remark 2.6.3: for a finite connected subgraph Γ′ ≠ {0} of the line graph containing 0, i.e. a vertex set [−a, b] ⊆ ℤ with max(a, b) ≥ 1, the file computes |Γ′| = min(max(a,b), l^⋇) and ‖Γ′‖ = (n + 1)(2n + 1)/6 with n = |Γ′|, so ‖Γ′‖ is a function of |Γ′|, strictly increasing in it, monotone in max(a,b), and maximal (value l (l + 1)/12) exactly when max(a,b) ≥ l^⋇. The paper calls this "easily verified"; the file verifies it.

Interpretation choices (recorded in data/curated/iut2.json, record IUT2.Rem2_6_3):

  • The paper assumes l ≥ 5 prime. Facts 1, 3, 4 are proved for every odd l ≥ 3; fact 2 for every odd prime.
  • An element of 𝔽_l^⋇ is represented by its unique representative c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} (justified by fact 1); its fiber in ℤ is {k | k ≡ c or k ≡ −c mod l}.
  • "Min over the empty set is zero" and "|Γ′| is the number of classes c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ hit by Γ′" are taken literally from page 79. Nonzero vertices divisible by l map to 0 ∉ 𝔽_l^⋇ and are not counted.
  • Page 80 (d) says "monotone increasing [but not strictly increasing!] function of |Γ′| … constant for |Γ′| ≥ l^⋇". Since |Γ′| ≤ l^⋇ by definition, the literal reading gives a strictly increasing function (gammaNormValue_strictMono); the parenthetical and the constancy match the reading in terms of the radius max(a,b) (gammaNorm_eq_max_iff, gammaNorm_mono_radius, gammaNorm_le_max). Both are proved; neither is imposed.
  • Infinite Γ′ (Remark 2.6.3 (iii)) is not treated.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.lstardef

def lstar (l : ℕ) : ℕ := (l - 1) / 2

l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 (IUT I, page 3).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.pmSetoiddef

def pmSetoid (l : ℕ) : Setoid (ZMod l) where
  r x y := x = y ∨ x = -y
  iseqv := by
    refine ⟨fun x => Or.inl rfl, ?_, ?_⟩
    · rintro x y (h | h)
      · exact Or.inl h.symm
      · exact Or.inr (by rw [h, neg_neg])
    · rintro x y z (h | h) (h' | h')
      · exact Or.inl (h.trans h')
      · exact Or.inr (h.trans h')
      · exact Or.inr (by rw [h, h'])
      · exact Or.inl (by rw [h, h', neg_neg])

The relation x ∼ y ⟺ x = y ∨ x = −y on 𝔽_l = ZMod l, whose quotient is |𝔽_l|.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.AbsLabeldef

def AbsLabel (l : ℕ) : Type := Quotient (pmSetoid l)

|𝔽_l| = 𝔽_l / {±1} (IUT II, page 3).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.labelRepdef

def labelRep (l : ℕ) (j : Fin (lstar l + 1)) : AbsLabel l :=

IUT.PaperII.Labels.FlStarabbrev

abbrev FlStar (l : ℕ) : Type := (ZMod l)ˣ ⧸ Subgroup.zpowers (-1 : (ZMod l)ˣ)

IUT.PaperII.Labels.six_mul_sum_sqtheorem

theorem six_mul_sum_sq (n : ℕ) : 6 * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 n, j ^ 2 = n * (n + 1) * (2 * n + 1)

6 · ∑_{j=1}^{n} j² = n (n + 1) (2n + 1).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.sum_exponentstheorem

theorem sum_exponents {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) :
  24 * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), j ^ 2 = l * (l ^ 2 - 1)

The exponents j², j = 1, …, l^⋇, of the Gaussian monoid (IUT II, pages 122, 136) satisfy 24 · ∑ j² = l (l² − 1) for odd l. Elementary; not stated in the paper.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.average_exponentstheorem

theorem average_exponents {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) :
  (∑ j ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (j : ℚ) ^ 2) / lstar l = l * (l + 1) / 12

With weight 1/l^⋇ at each j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ (IUT II, Remark 4.7.3 (iii), page 145), the weighted total of the exponents j² is l (l + 1)/12, for odd l ≥ 3. Elementary; not stated in the paper.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.fiberIndef

def fiberIn (l a b c : ℕ) : Finset ℤ :=
  (Icc (-(a : ℤ)) b).filter fun k => (k : ZMod l) = (c : ZMod l) ∨ (k : ZMod l) = -(c : ZMod l)

The intersection of Γ′ = [−a, b] with the fiber over the class of c of the projection ℤ → |𝔽_l|, i.e. the integers k ∈ [−a, b] with k ≡ ±c (mod l).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.minSqdef

def minSq (S : Finset ℤ) : ℕ :=
  if h : S.Nonempty then (S.image fun k => k.natAbs ^ 2).min' (h.image _) else 0

min_{k ∈ S} k², with the convention of page 79 that the min over the empty set is 0.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCarddef

def gammaCard (l a b : ℕ) : ℕ := ((Icc 1 (lstar l)).filter fun c => (fiberIn l a b c).Nonempty).card

|Γ′|: the number of classes c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ (represented by c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇}) that contain a vertex of Γ′ = [−a, b], i.e. the size of the image in 𝔽_l^⋇ of the nonzero vertices.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormdef

def gammaNorm (l a b : ℕ) : ℚ :=
  (∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (minSq (fiberIn l a b c) : ℚ)) / gammaCard l a b

‖Γ′‖ = |Γ′|⁻¹ · ∑_{c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{k ∈ c ∩ Γ′} k² (IUT II, page 79). Domain: the paper's formula has a meaning only when |Γ′| ≥ 1, that is Γ′ ≠ {0} (one_le_gammaCard_iff). For a = b = 0 Lean's division by zero makes this function return 0; that value is a convention of the Lean definition, not a value given on page 79. Every lemma below that evaluates gammaNorm assumes 1 ≤ gammaCard l a b or 1 ≤ max a b.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.le_natAbs_of_mem_fiberlemma

lemma le_natAbs_of_mem_fiber {l c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) {k : ℤ}
  (hk : (k : ZMod l) = (c : ZMod l) ∨ (k : ZMod l) = -(c : ZMod l)) : c ≤ k.natAbs

A vertex in the fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} has absolute value at least c.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.fiberIn_nonempty_ifflemma

lemma fiberIn_nonempty_iff {l a b c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) :
  (fiberIn l a b c).Nonempty ↔ c ≤ max a b

The fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} meets [−a, b] if and only if c ≤ max a b.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.minSq_fiberInlemma

lemma minSq_fiberIn {l a b c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) (h : c ≤ max a b) :
  minSq (fiberIn l a b c) = c ^ 2

When the fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} meets [−a, b], the minimum of k² over it is c².

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCard_eqtheorem

theorem gammaCard_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) :
  gammaCard l a b = min (max a b) (lstar l)

|Γ′| = min(max(a, b), l^⋇).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.sum_minSq_eqtheorem

theorem sum_minSq_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) :
  ∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (minSq (fiberIn l a b c) : ℚ) =
    ∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (gammaCard l a b), (c : ℚ) ^ 2

∑_{c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{k ∈ c ∩ Γ′} k² = ∑_{c = 1}^{|Γ′|} c².

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValuedef

def gammaNormValue (n : ℕ) : ℚ := ((n : ℚ) + 1) * (2 * n + 1) / 6

The closed form g(n) = (n + 1)(2n + 1)/6.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_eqtheorem

theorem gammaNorm_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) (hpos : 1 ≤ gammaCard l a b) :
  gammaNorm l a b = gammaNormValue (gammaCard l a b)

Remark 2.6.3 (d), first part: ‖Γ′‖ is a function of |Γ′| alone, namely ‖Γ′‖ = (|Γ′| + 1)(2|Γ′| + 1)/6, whenever |Γ′| ≥ 1.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.one_le_gammaCard_ifftheorem

theorem one_le_gammaCard_iff {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) :
  1 ≤ gammaCard l a b ↔ 1 ≤ max a b

|Γ′| ≥ 1 exactly when Γ′ ≠ {0}, for l ≥ 3.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValue_strictMonotheorem

theorem gammaNormValue_strictMono : StrictMono gammaNormValue

The literal reading of (d): as a function of |Γ′|, the value ‖Γ′‖ is strictly increasing. Since |Γ′| ≤ l^⋇, its maximum is attained at |Γ′| = l^⋇.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCard_letheorem

theorem gammaCard_le {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) : gammaCard l a b ≤ lstar l

|Γ′| ≤ l^⋇.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValue_lstartheorem

theorem gammaNormValue_lstar {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) :
  gammaNormValue (lstar l) = l * (l + 1) / 12

The maximal value: g(l^⋇) = l (l + 1)/12 for odd l.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_le_maxtheorem

theorem gammaNorm_le_max {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) (hab : 1 ≤ max a b) :
  gammaNorm l a b ≤ l * (l + 1) / 12

The radius reading of (d): ‖Γ′‖ ≤ l (l + 1)/12 for every Γ′ = [−a, b] ≠ {0}.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_eq_max_ifftheorem

theorem gammaNorm_eq_max_iff {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) (hab : 1 ≤ max a b) :
  gammaNorm l a b = l * (l + 1) / 12 ↔ lstar l ≤ max a b

The radius reading of (d): the maximum l (l + 1)/12 is attained exactly when max(a, b) ≥ l^⋇, so ‖Γ′‖ is constant in the radius from l^⋇ on.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_mono_radiustheorem

theorem gammaNorm_mono_radius {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) {a b a' b' : ℕ}
  (hab : 1 ≤ max a b) (h : max a b ≤ max a' b') : gammaNorm l a b ≤ gammaNorm l a' b'

The radius reading of (d): ‖Γ′‖ is monotone in the radius max(a, b).

IUT III

Canonical splittings of the log-theta-lattice.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Comparison

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/Comparison.lean · imports IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Numerical, IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.HullOperator

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: an interface for the final comparison of regions

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Corollary 3.12 and Steps (x) to (xii) of its proof, pages 173 to 186; Proposition 3.9, pages 115 to 118; Remark 3.9.5 (i) to (iv), pages 126 to 129. Pages read for this file from the extracted text: 115 to 118, 125 to 129, 173 to 175, 180 to 186. Pages checked against rendered images: 115 to 117, 127, 173 to 175, 181 to 185. Pages 118, 125, 126, 128, 129, 180 and 186 were read in text only; no formula of this file is taken from them.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12. It is an interface for the last step of the proof, the passage from regions to the two numbers − |log(Θ)| and − |log(q)|. It does not construct any region from initial Θ-data, Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice, the log-Kummer correspondence or the multiradial representation of Theorem 3.11. All such regions are inputs.

The comparison of the q-pilot region with the hull of the union of the possible images (Containment, LogVolumeComparison below) is, in the paper, the content of Theorem 3.11 together with Steps (i) to (xi) of the proof. Here it is an unproved hypothesis. Every theorem below that takes it as a hypothesis is conditional on it, and nothing in this file gives evidence for or against it.

What is typed
  • logVolume μ S = ENNReal.log (μ S): the log-volume of a region, with log 0 = −∞ and log ∞ = +∞ (Mathlib's ENNReal.log). It is monotone, and finite exactly when 0 < μ S < ∞.
  • ComparisonSetting X ι: data only. A measure μ on a measurable space X, a family 𝓟 of regions on which a hull is defined, a family 𝓗 of hulls, a hull map with the properties (P1), (P2), (P3) of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) (IsHullMap from HullOperator.lean), the q-pilot region, and a family of possible images indexed by an arbitrary type ι (the indeterminacy data). One regularity field: the union of the possible images lies in 𝓟.
  • Containment (region form) and LogVolumeComparison (numerical form) of the comparison, as separate Props on a setting; logVolumeComparison_of_containment.
  • The bridge to Numerical.lean: toPilotLogVolumes and conclusion_of_containment.
  • Averaging over a finite set of labels j with equal weights (average, conclusion_of_procContainment).
  • Nonvacuity on ℝ with Lebesgue measure and the interval hull Icc (sInf P) (sSup P): a setting where every hypothesis of the bridge holds (example_bridge_hypotheses), one where all of them except the comparison hold and the conclusion fails (example_comparison_fails), and one where no single possible image, and not the union either, contains the q-pilot region but the hull of the union does (example_union_matters).
Interpretation choices
  1. Regions and log-volume. Proposition 3.9 (i), pages 115 and 116, defines log-volumes on nonempty compact open subsets (nonarchimedean) or compact closures of nonempty open subsets (archimedean), normalized so that the integral structure has log-volume 0 and multiplication by p_v adds −log(p_v) (multiplication by e adds 1). Here a region is an arbitrary subset and the log-volume is log of an arbitrary measure. The normalization "integral structure has log-volume 0" is not part of the setting; it enters only through the hypothesis μ qPilot < 1, which is what |log(q)| > 0 (page 174) says once the log-volume is log μ.
  2. The domain of the hull. Remark 3.9.5 (i), page 127, defines the hull of any subset that contains a relatively compact subset of finite log-volume; (ii) states (P1) to (P3) on the set ℙ of direct product regions. The paper does not say that the union of the possible images is a direct product region. The field union_mem takes 𝓟 to be a family on which (P1) to (P3) hold and which contains the union; this is a choice.
  3. Possible images. Page 174 defines n,◦U_{j,v_Q} as the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object subject to (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3), without a formula for the set of possible images. Here that set is an arbitrary indexed family image : ι → Set X; how (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3) generate it is not modeled.
  4. Signs. The symbol − |log(Θ)| is a name for the log-volume of the hull of the union, not the negative of an absolute value: negLogTheta is that log-volume itself. The symbol − |log(q)| is the log-volume of the q-pilot region, so absLogQ is minus that log-volume.
  5. Which form the text asserts. In the reading adopted here, the text never asserts a containment of regions. Steps (xi-c) and (xi-d), pages 182 and 183, say that the possible output data in 1,◦Ū are "linked/related" to the representation of the q-pilot object via isomorphisms of F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips. Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts the numerical inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}. So LogVolumeComparison is what the text asserts, and Containment is a region-level sufficient condition, not a quotation.
  6. Averaging. The statement (page 173) and Step (x) (page 181, rendered) average over j ∈ 𝔽_l^⊛; page 174 indexes the regions by j ∈ |𝔽_l|, and Step (xi-d), page 183, normalizes "with respect to j ∈ |𝔽_l|". Proposition 3.9 (i), page 116, defines the procession-normalization as "the average, over the various capsules". The average here is uniform over an arbitrary nonempty finite index type J; the statement itself averages over 𝔽_l^⊛ with factor 1 / l^⊛ (pages 173 and 181); the file keeps J general.
What the interface omits, and what could be added next
  • *The sum over places v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ* (Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117). Elementary to add for a finite set of places, in the same way as the average over j (a sum with weight 1); not added, because the weights of Remark 3.1.1 (ii) to (iv) were not read.
  • The procession structure. The labels j index capsules of a procession, and the packet at j is a tensor product over j + 1 labels. Only the equal-weight average over j is here.
  • *The identification of the two sides through the Θ^{×μ}_{LGP}-link* (Step (xi-a), page
    1. and through the permutation symmetry 0,◦U ≅ 1,◦U (Step (xi-b), page 181). Here both regions live in one space X with one measure μ; that they can be placed there with a common measure is assumed by the typing, not proved. This is where IUT.Countermodels.Comparison shows some compatibility is indispensable.
  • Tensor packets, determinants and the tensor power of Step (xi-d), page 183; not modeled.
  • The indeterminacies themselves. The invariance of log-volume under (Ind1), (Ind2) and the inequality from (Ind3) (Step (x), page 181) are not stated; the family image is arbitrary. A next step is to generate image as {Y | ∃ g ∈ G, g • R ⊑ Y} for a group G of measure preserving maps and a containment relation ⊑, and to prove the invariance.
