IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolumedef

noncomputable def logVolume (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) : EReal := ENNReal.log (μ S)

The log-volume of a region S relative to a measure μ: log (μ S), with value −∞ when μ S = 0 and +∞ when μ S = ∞.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_monotheorem

theorem logVolume_mono (μ : Measure X) {S T : Set X} (h : S ⊆ T) :
  logVolume μ S ≤ logVolume μ T

Log-volume is monotone with respect to inclusion of regions.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_eq_bot_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_eq_bot_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S = ⊥ ↔ μ S = 0

The log-volume is −∞ exactly when the region has measure zero.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_eq_top_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_eq_top_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S = ⊤ ↔ μ S = ⊤

The log-volume is +∞ exactly when the region has infinite measure.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_finite_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_finite_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  (logVolume μ S ≠ ⊥ ∧ logVolume μ S ≠ ⊤) ↔ (μ S ≠ 0 ∧ μ S ≠ ⊤)

The log-volume is a real number exactly when the measure lies in (0, ∞).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_neg_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_neg_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S < 0 ↔ μ S < 1

The log-volume is negative exactly when the measure is less than 1.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSettingstructure

structure ComparisonSetting (X : Type*) [MeasurableSpace X] (ι : Type*) where

The data for the last step of the proof of Corollary 3.12, for one component. This is not the paper's situation: none of the fields is constructed from Hodge theaters or the multiradial representation. The fields are data, plus the hull axioms and one well-posedness condition. No field relates the q-pilot region to the possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.unionImagesdef

def unionImages : Set X := ⋃ i, S.image i

The union of the possible images (n,◦U, page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.hullOfUniondef

def hullOfUnion : Set X := S.hull S.unionImages

The hull of the union of the possible images (n,◦Ū, pages 174 and 175).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.thetaLogVolumedef

noncomputable def thetaLogVolume : EReal := logVolume S.μ S.hullOfUnion

The log-volume of the hull of the union: the quantity named − |log(Θ)| (page 173).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.qLogVolumedef

noncomputable def qLogVolume : EReal := logVolume S.μ S.qPilot

The log-volume of the q-pilot region: the quantity named − |log(q)| (page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.image_subset_hullOfUniontheorem

theorem image_subset_hullOfUnion (i : ι) : S.image i ⊆ S.hullOfUnion

Each possible image lies in the hull of the union, by (P2).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.Containmentdef

def Containment : Prop := S.qPilot ⊆ S.hullOfUnion

Comparison, region form. The q-pilot region is contained in the hull of the union of the possible images. In the reading adopted here the text does not assert this; see LogVolumeComparison. It is a region-level condition that implies the numerical form (logVolumeComparison_of_containment). Its proof in the paper, in whatever form, would rest on Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi); it is not formalized here.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.LogVolumeComparisondef

def LogVolumeComparison : Prop := S.qLogVolume ≤ S.thetaLogVolume

Comparison, numerical form. − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)|, i.e. the log-volume of the q-pilot region is at most that of the hull of the union. This is what Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts ("the inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}"). Its derivation in the paper rests on Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi-e); it is not formalized here. Once the two numbers are defined from a setting, this is the second conjunct of Conclusion (see conclusion_iff), so assuming it is assuming that conjunct.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.logVolumeComparison_of_containmenttheorem

theorem logVolumeComparison_of_containment (h : S.Containment) : S.LogVolumeComparison

The region form implies the numerical form, by monotonicity of log-volume.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.containment_iff_forall_hulltheorem

theorem containment_iff_forall_hull :
  S.Containment ↔ ∀ H ∈ S.hulls, (∀ i, S.image i ⊆ H) → S.qPilot ⊆ H

The region form, through the hull axioms: the q-pilot region lies in the hull of the union exactly when it lies in every hull that contains all possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes (a b : EReal) (ha : a ≠ ⊥) (hneg : a < 0)
    (hb : b ≠ ⊥) : PilotLogVolumes where
  negLogTheta := b
  negLogTheta_ne_bot := hb
  absLogQ := -a.toReal
  absLogQ_pos := by
    have hcoe := EReal.coe_toReal (hneg.trans EReal.zero_lt_top).ne ha
    rw [← hcoe] at hneg
    linarith [EReal.coe_lt_coe_iff.mp (hneg.trans_eq (EReal.coe_zero).symm)]

Two log-volumes a (the q side) and b (the Θ side) as a PilotLogVolumes: negLogTheta = b and absLogQ = −a. The hypotheses are the facts PilotLogVolumes requires: b ≠ −∞ (value set ℝ ∪ {+∞}, page 173) and a real and negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumes.conclusion_ofLogVolumes_ifftheorem

theorem PilotLogVolumes.conclusion_ofLogVolumes_iff (a b : EReal) (ha : a ≠ ⊥) (hneg : a < 0)
  (hb : b ≠ ⊥) : Conclusion (ofLogVolumes a b ha hneg hb) ↔ b ≠ ⊤ ∧ a ≤ b

For numbers built by ofLogVolumes, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is exactly finiteness of b together with a ≤ b.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.toPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def toPilotLogVolumes (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
    (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) : PilotLogVolumes :=
  PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes S.qLogVolume S.thetaLogVolume
    ((logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr hq0) ((logVolume_neg_iff _ _).mpr hq1)
    ((logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr hΘ)

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 defined from a setting: − |log(Θ)| is the log-volume of the hull of the union, |log(q)| is minus the log-volume of the q-pilot region. Hypotheses: hq0, hq1: 0 < μ qPilot < 1, i.e. − |log(q)| is real and negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174); hΘ: the hull of the union has positive measure, i.e. − |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞ (value set ℝ ∪ {+∞}, page 173).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_ifftheorem

theorem conclusion_iff (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ) ↔
    S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧ S.LogVolumeComparison

For the numbers defined from a setting, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is exactly finiteness of the hull of the union together with LogVolumeComparison. This makes explicit that the numerical form of the comparison is the inequality of the conclusion itself.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_of_logVolumeComparisontheorem

theorem conclusion_of_logVolumeComparison (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) (hfin : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤)
  (hcomp : S.LogVolumeComparison) : Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ)

Bridge, numerical form. LogVolumeComparison and finiteness give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12. This is an unfolding of definitions (conclusion_iff), recorded because the task of the last step is exactly to supply these two hypotheses. * hfin: the hull of the union has finite measure. The paper argues this on page 175, from "the [easily verified] compactness" of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q}; not formalized. * hcomp: asserted by Step (xi-f), page 184, from Theorem 3.11; not formalized.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_of_containmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_containment (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hfin : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤) (hcont : S.Containment) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1
    (fun h => hq0 (measure_mono_null hcont h)))

Bridge, region form (conditional). If the q-pilot region is contained in the hull of the union of the possible images, and that hull has finite measure, then the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 holds for the numbers defined from the setting. Conditional on two unproved hypotheses: * hcont: the region form of the comparison; in the paper, the content of Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi) (the text asserts the numerical form, Step (xi-f), page 184); * hfin: finiteness, argued on page 175 from compactness of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q}. The hypothesis hΘ (− |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞) is not needed: it follows from hcont and 0 < μ qPilot.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.averagedef

noncomputable def average (v : J → EReal) : EReal :=
  (((Fintype.card J : ℝ)⁻¹ : ℝ) : EReal) * ∑ j, v j

The equal-weight average (1/|J|) · ∑_j v_j of a finite family of extended reals: the procession-normalization of Proposition 3.9 (i), page 116 ("the average, over the various capsules").

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.average_monotheorem

theorem average_mono {v w : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≤ w j) : average v ≤ average w

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.average_ne_bottheorem

theorem average_ne_bot {v : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≠ ⊥) : average v ≠ ⊥

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.average_ne_toptheorem

theorem average_ne_top {v : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≠ ⊤) : average v ≠ ⊤

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procThetaLogVolumedef

noncomputable def procThetaLogVolume : EReal := average fun j => (S j).thetaLogVolume

The averaged log-volume of the hulls of the unions: − |log(Θ)| with the procession-normalization.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procQLogVolumedef

noncomputable def procQLogVolume : EReal := average fun j => (S j).qLogVolume

The averaged log-volume of the q-pilot regions: − |log(q)| with the procession-normalization.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ProcContainmentdef

def ProcContainment : Prop := ∀ j, (S j).Containment

Region form of the comparison for a family of components: componentwise containment. Page 174 writes regions as collections indexed by (j, v_Q) and their containment componentwise. Not proved here; see ComparisonSetting.Containment.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ProcLogVolumeComparisondef

def ProcLogVolumeComparison : Prop := procQLogVolume S ≤ procThetaLogVolume S

Numerical form of the comparison with the procession-normalization: − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)| for the averaged log-volumes (Step (xi-f), page 184).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procLogVolumeComparison_of_procContainmenttheorem

theorem procLogVolumeComparison_of_procContainment (h : ProcContainment S) :
  ProcLogVolumeComparison S

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def procPilotLogVolumes (hq : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
      (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ ⊤) (hneg : procQLogVolume S < 0)
    (hΘ : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) : PilotLogVolumes :=
  PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes (procQLogVolume S) (procThetaLogVolume S)
    (average_ne_bot fun j => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hq j).1) hneg
    (average_ne_bot fun j => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hΘ j))

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 with the procession-normalization. Hypotheses: each q-pilot component has measure in (0, ∞) and the averaged q-side log-volume is negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174); each hull component has positive measure.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_procContainmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_procContainment (hq : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
    (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ ⊤) (hneg : procQLogVolume S < 0)
  (hΘ : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) (hfin : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤)
  (hcont : ProcContainment S) : Conclusion (procPilotLogVolumes S hq hneg hΘ)

Bridge with averaging (conditional). Componentwise containment of the q-pilot regions in the hulls of the unions, and finiteness of each hull, give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the procession-normalized numbers. Conditional on hcont (Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi), not formalized) and hfin (page 175, compactness, not formalized).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.domaindef

def domain : Set (Set ℝ) := {P | P.Nonempty ∧ BddBelow P ∧ BddAbove P}

Bounded nonempty subsets of ℝ: the domain of the interval hull.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hullsdef

def hulls : Set (Set ℝ) := {H | ∃ a b, a ≤ b ∧ H = Icc a b}

Nonempty closed bounded intervals: the hulls.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hulldef

noncomputable def hull (P : Set ℝ) : Set ℝ := Icc (sInf P) (sSup P)

The interval hull P ↦ [inf P, sup P].

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.isHullMaptheorem

theorem isHullMap : IsHullMap domain hulls hull

The interval hull satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3) of Remark 3.9.5 (ii).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.imagedef

noncomputable def image : Bool → Set ℝ
  | false => Icc 0 (1 / 8)
  | true => Icc (3 / 8) (1 / 2)

The two possible images [0, 1/8] and [3/8, 1/2], indexed by Bool.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.union_eqtheorem

theorem union_eq : (⋃ i, image i) = Icc 0 (1 / 8) ∪ Icc (3 / 8) (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hull_uniontheorem

theorem hull_union : hull (⋃ i, image i) = Icc 0 (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.settingdef

noncomputable def setting (q : Set ℝ) : ComparisonSetting ℝ Bool where
  μ := volume
  hullDomain := domain
  hulls := hulls
  hull := hull
  isHullMap := isHullMap
  qPilot := q
  image := image
  union_mem := by
    rw [union_eq]
    exact ⟨⟨0, Or.inl ⟨le_rfl, by norm_num⟩⟩, bddBelow_Icc.union bddBelow_Icc,
      bddAbove_Icc.union bddAbove_Icc⟩

The setting on ℝ with Lebesgue measure, the interval hull, the two possible images above, and a q-pilot region q.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.setting_hullOfUniontheorem

theorem setting_hullOfUnion (q : Set ℝ) : (setting q).hullOfUnion = Icc 0 (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.volume_hullOfUniontheorem

theorem volume_hullOfUnion (q : Set ℝ) :
  (setting q).μ (setting q).hullOfUnion = ENNReal.ofReal (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_bridge_hypothesestheorem

theorem example_bridge_hypotheses :
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).qPilot < 1 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).hullOfUnion ≠ 0 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).Containment

Nonvacuity (a). With q-pilot region [0, 1/2], every hypothesis of the region-form bridge holds: 0 < μ q < 1, the hull of the union has finite positive measure, and the q-pilot region is contained in it. Hence conclusion_of_containment applies.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_conclusiontheorem

theorem example_conclusion :
  ∃ v : PilotLogVolumes, Conclusion v

The conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the numbers of example (a), obtained from the bridge.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_comparison_failstheorem

theorem example_comparison_fails :
  ∃ (hq0 : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).qPilot ≠ 0)
    (hq1 : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).qPilot < 1)
    (hΘ : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).hullOfUnion ≠ 0),
    (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧
    ¬ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).LogVolumeComparison ∧
    ¬ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).Containment ∧
    ¬ Conclusion ((setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ)

Nonvacuity (b): the comparison is an extra condition. With q-pilot region [0, 3/4], every field of the setting and every side hypothesis of the bridge holds (0 < μ q < 1, the hull of the union has finite positive measure), but LogVolumeComparison fails, hence also Containment, and the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is false for the numbers so defined.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_union_matterstheorem

theorem example_union_matters :
  (∀ i, ¬ Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2) ⊆ hull (image i)) ∧
  (∀ i, ¬ logVolume volume (Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2)) ≤ logVolume volume (hull (image i))) ∧
  ¬ Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2) ⊆ ⋃ i, image i ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).Containment

Nonvacuity (c): the union and the hull matter. With q-pilot region [0, 1/2]: the hull of no single possible image contains the q-pilot region, nor does any single image satisfy the numerical comparison with its own hull; the union itself does not contain the q-pilot region; but the hull of the union does.

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: the numerical layer of the statement

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Corollary 3.12 and its proof, pages 173 to 186 of the May 2020 manuscript, read in full. The statement was also read from the rendered pages 173 and 174.

What this file is and is not

Corollary 3.12 defines two numbers from a collection of initial Θ-data:

  • − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ ∪ {+∞}, the procession-normalized mono-analytic log-volume of the holomorphic hull of the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object, subject to the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3);
  • − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ, the corresponding log-volume of the image of a q-pilot object, not subject to the indeterminacies, with |log(q)| > 0.

It asserts that − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ and − |log(Θ)| ≥ − |log(q)|.

This project does not yet construct either number from initial Θ-data: Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice, tensor packets and the multiradial representation are not formalized. So this file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12. It formalizes only what can be said about the two numbers once they are given: the shape of the conclusion, the "i.e." reformulation in the statement, the reduction at the start of the proof, and the purely numerical content of Steps (xi-d) to (xi-f). The structure below has no field that assumes any conclusion of the paper.

Public declarations
variable (v : PilotLogVolumes)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumesstructure

structure PilotLogVolumes where

The two numbers that the statement of Corollary 3.12 defines, with the two properties that the statement attributes to them by definition: the value set ℝ ∪ {+∞} of − |log(Θ)|, and |log(q)| > 0. Nothing here is constructed from initial Θ-data.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Conclusiondef

def Conclusion : Prop :=
  v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤ ∧ ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

The conclusion of Corollary 3.12: − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ and − |log(Θ)| ≥ − |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ConstantFormdef

def ConstantForm : Prop :=
  ∀ C : ℝ, v.negLogTheta ≤ ((C * v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) → -1 ≤ C

The reformulation after "i.e." in the statement: C_Θ ≥ −1 for any real number C_Θ such that − |log(Θ)| ≤ C_Θ · |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.constantForm_of_conclusiontheorem

theorem constantForm_of_conclusion (h : Conclusion v) : ConstantForm v

The conclusion implies the "i.e." form.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_constantFormtheorem

theorem conclusion_of_constantForm (hfin : v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤) (h : ConstantForm v) :
  Conclusion v

When − |log(Θ)| is finite, the "i.e." form implies the inequality.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.constantForm_of_toptheorem

theorem constantForm_of_top (h : v.negLogTheta = ⊤) : ConstantForm v

The "i.e." form alone does not give finiteness: it holds when − |log(Θ)| = +∞, because then no real C_Θ satisfies its hypothesis. So the "i.e." of the statement reformulates the inequality, and the assertion − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ is a separate claim.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_iff_constantFormtheorem

theorem conclusion_iff_constantForm (hfin : v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤) :
  Conclusion v ↔ ConstantForm v

With finiteness assumed, the conclusion and the "i.e." form are equivalent.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.inequality_of_nonnegtheorem

theorem inequality_of_nonneg (h : 0 ≤ v.negLogTheta) :
  ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

The reduction at the start of the proof: "we may assume without loss of generality that − |log(Θ)| < 0", because − |log(Θ)| ≥ 0 gives − |log(Θ)| ≥ 0 > − |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.possibleLogVolumesdef

def possibleLogVolumes : Set ℝ := {lam | (lam : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta}

The subset ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|} = {λ ∈ ℝ | λ ≤ − |log(Θ)|} of Step (xi-d).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.stepXIf_inclusion_iff_inequalitytheorem

theorem stepXIf_inclusion_iff_inequality :
  -v.absLogQ ∈ possibleLogVolumes v ↔ ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

Step (xi-f) at the level of numbers. The proof ends: "The inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}, hence also the inequality − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)|, then follows formally." The second step is indeed formal: the inclusion and the inequality are the same proposition, by unfolding the definition of the subset. Consequently every bit of mathematical content of Step (xi) lies in establishing the inclusion, that is, in Steps (xi-a) to (xi-e) and the first sentence of (xi-f). No formalization that keeps only the two real numbers can express those steps: stated about numbers alone, the premise of the last step is the conclusion. A non-circular formalization must model the multiradial representation itself, with the properties (IPL) and (SHE) of Remark 3.11.1.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.thm1_10_final_of_cor312theorem

theorem thm1_10_final_of_cor312 {l d D E Q : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hE : 0 ≤ E)
  (hbound : v.negLogTheta ≤ ((constant l d D E Q v.absLogQ * v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal))
  (hcor : Conclusion v) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E

Conditional derivation of the final inequality of IUT IV, Theorem 1.10. Hypotheses, each a statement about given real numbers: * hbound: − |log(Θ)| ≤ C_Θ · |log(q)| for the constant C_Θ displayed in Theorem 1.10. This is the first assertion of Theorem 1.10 ("one may take the constant C_Θ to be ..."). * hcor: the conclusion of Corollary 3.12. * the side conditions 7 ≤ l, 1 ≤ d, 0 ≤ D, 0 ≤ E that the paper cites. Conclusion: (1/6)·log(𝔮) ≤ (1 + 20·d_mod/l)·(log 𝔡 + log 𝔣) + 20·(e*_mod·l + η_prm). The theorem is conditional. It does not show that the objects of the paper satisfy hbound or hcor; it shows that nothing else is used in passing from them to the final inequality.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.HullOperator

IUT/PaperIII/Remark3_9_5/HullOperator.lean

IUT III, Remark 3.9.5 (ii): the order-theoretic characterization of the hull map

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 3.9.5, (i), (ii), pages 126 to 128. Pages 126 to 145 (all of Remark 3.9.5) were read from the extracted text; page 127, which carries the displays of (i) and (ii), was checked against a rendered image.

What the paper says

Remark 3.9.5 (i) defines the holomorphic hull of a suitable subset U of a tensor packet ℐ^ℚ(−) as the smallest subset of the form λ · 𝒪 containing U. Remark 3.9.5 (ii) writes ℙ for the set of direct product regions and ℍ ⊆ ℙ for the set of hulls, and asserts that forming the hull is a map φ : ℙ → ℍ that "may be characterized uniquely" by

  • (P1) φ(H) = H for every H ∈ ℍ;
  • (P2) P ⊆ φ(P) for every P ∈ ℙ;
  • (P3) φ(P₁) ⊆ φ(P₂) whenever P₁ ⊆ P₂ in ℙ.

It then says that, since any intersection of elements of ℍ which is itself of finite log-volume necessarily determines an element of ℍ (the qualifier "of finite log-volume" applies to the intersection, not to the members intersected), it "follows formally" from (P1), (P2), (P3) that φ(P) is the intersection of all H ∈ ℍ containing P.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of holomorphic hulls or of Remark 3.9.5. Tensor packets, log-shells and direct product regions are not formalized. It checks the order-theoretic part of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) for arbitrary families 𝓗 ⊆ 𝓟 of subsets of an arbitrary type:

  • IsHullMap.eq_sInter: (P1), (P2), (P3), together with ℍ ⊆ ℙ and φ(ℙ) ⊆ ℍ (the two other clauses of IsHullMap, both stated on page 127), force φ(P) = ⋂ {H ∈ 𝓗 | P ⊆ H}. The intersection hypothesis quoted by the paper is not needed for this formula.
  • IsHullMap.unique: hence at most one map satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3); this is the paper's "characterized uniquely".
  • isHullMap_sInter: if for each P ∈ 𝓟 the intersection of the members of 𝓗 containing P lies in 𝓗, then that intersection defines a map satisfying (P1), (P2), (P3). This is the role of the intersection hypothesis: it gives existence, not the formula.

The paper's closure claim (an intersection of hulls that has finite log-volume is a hull) is replaced by the plain hypothesis hcl of isHullMap_sInter: for each P ∈ 𝓟 the intersection of the members of 𝓗 containing P lies in 𝓗. That hypothesis is a statement about the given families only; no log-volume occurs in this file, and the finite-volume condition of the paper is not modeled.

Public declarations
variable {X : Type*}
variable {𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)} {φ : Set X → Set X}

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMapstructure

structure IsHullMap (𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)) (φ : Set X → Set X) : Prop where

A map φ on subsets of X satisfies the conditions of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) relative to the families 𝓟 (playing the role of direct product regions) and 𝓗 (playing the role of hulls): it sends 𝓟 into 𝓗, and satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3).

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMap.eq_sIntertheorem

theorem eq_sInter (h : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 φ) {P : Set X} (hP : P ∈ 𝓟) :
  φ P = ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H}

The clauses of IsHullMap (ℍ ⊆ ℙ, φ(ℙ) ⊆ ℍ, (P1), (P2), (P3)) force φ(P) = ⋂ {H ∈ ℍ | P ⊆ H}, with no hypothesis on intersections.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMap.uniquetheorem

theorem unique {ψ : Set X → Set X} (hφ : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 φ) (hψ : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 ψ)
  {P : Set X} (hP : P ∈ 𝓟) : φ P = ψ P

At most one map satisfies the conditions of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) on ℙ.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.isHullMap_sIntertheorem

theorem isHullMap_sInter {𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)} (hsub : 𝓗 ⊆ 𝓟)
  (hcl : ∀ P ∈ 𝓟, ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H} ∈ 𝓗) :
  IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 (fun P => ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H})

Existence: if ℍ ⊆ ℙ and, for each P ∈ ℙ, the intersection of the members of ℍ containing P lies in ℍ, then P ↦ ⋂ {H ∈ ℍ | P ⊆ H} satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3).

IUT IV

Log-volume computations and set-theoretic foundations.

IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

IUT/PaperIV/ArithmeticDivisor.lean

IUT IV, Definition 1.9 (i): arithmetic divisors on a number field

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, pages 21 and 22 of the April 2020 manuscript, which recalls notation from [GenEll], §1.

For a number field F, the set 𝕍(F) of valuations is the disjoint union of the nonarchimedean ones and the archimedean ones. An ℝ-arithmetic divisor is a finite formal sum ∑ c_v · v with real coefficients. The degree map sends a nonarchimedean v to log(q_v), with q_v the cardinality of the residue field, and an archimedean v to 1.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • Nonarchimedean valuations are represented by the nonzero prime ideals of the ring of integers (IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F)), archimedean ones by NumberField.InfinitePlace F. A pair of complex conjugate embeddings is one valuation.
  • The archimedean weight is 1 for real and complex places alike, exactly as in the display of Definition 1.9 (i). Other conventions weight a complex place by 2.
  • The pull-back 𝔞|_K to a finite extension, and the identity deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) for the normalized degree, are stated in the paper "in the evident fashion" and are not formalized here. Part (ii) of the definition is not formalized here either.
Public declarations
variable (F : Type*) [Field F] [NumberField F]
variable {F}

IUT.PaperIV.Valuationsabbrev

abbrev Valuations : Type _ :=
  IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F) ⊕ InfinitePlace F

The set 𝕍(F) of valuations of F: nonarchimedean ones on the left, archimedean ones on the right.

IUT.PaperIV.ADivabbrev

abbrev ADiv : Type _ := Valuations F →₀ ℝ

The group ADiv_ℝ(F) of ℝ-arithmetic divisors: finite formal real combinations of valuations.

IUT.PaperIV.degWeightdef

noncomputable def degWeight : Valuations F → ℝ
  | .inl v => Real.log (Ideal.absNorm v.asIdeal)
  | .inr _ => 1

The weight of a valuation in the degree map: log(q_v) at a nonarchimedean valuation, where q_v is the cardinality of the residue field, and 1 at an archimedean one.

IUT.PaperIV.degWeight_nonnegtheorem

theorem degWeight_nonneg (v : Valuations F) : 0 ≤ degWeight v

IUT.PaperIV.ADiv.suppdef

def ADiv.supp (a : ADiv F) : Finset (Valuations F) := a.support

The support Supp(𝔞): the valuations with nonzero coefficient.

IUT.PaperIV.ADiv.Effectivedef

def ADiv.Effective (a : ADiv F) : Prop := ∀ v, 0 ≤ a v

An arithmetic divisor is effective when all its coefficients are ≥ 0.

IUT.PaperIV.degFdef

noncomputable def degF : ADiv F →+ ℝ :=
  Finsupp.liftAddHom fun v => AddMonoidHom.mulLeft (degWeight v)

The degree map deg_F : ADiv_ℝ(F) → ℝ, a homomorphism of groups.

IUT.PaperIV.degF_singletheorem

theorem degF_single (v : Valuations F) (c : ℝ) :
  degF (Finsupp.single v c) = degWeight v * c

IUT.PaperIV.degF_applytheorem

theorem degF_apply (a : ADiv F) : degF a = a.sum fun v c => degWeight v * c

IUT.PaperIV.degF_nonnegtheorem

theorem degF_nonneg {a : ADiv F} (h : a.Effective) : 0 ≤ degF a

An effective arithmetic divisor has nonnegative degree.

IUT.PaperIV.normalizedDegdef

noncomputable def normalizedDeg (a : ADiv F) : ℝ := degF a / Module.finrank ℚ F

The normalized degree deg(𝔞) = deg_F(𝔞) / [F : ℚ].

IUT.PaperIV.normalizedDeg_addtheorem

theorem normalizedDeg_add (a b : ADiv F) :
  normalizedDeg (a + b) = normalizedDeg a + normalizedDeg b

IUT.PaperIV.normalizedDeg_nonnegtheorem

theorem normalizedDeg_nonneg {a : ADiv F} (h : a.Effective) : 0 ≤ normalizedDeg a

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.Arithmetic

IUT/PaperIV/Corollary2_2/Arithmetic.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.ElementaryFacts, IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1

IUT IV, Corollary 2.2: the real arithmetic of (C1), (C2) and of the proof

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Corollary 2.2 and its proof, pages 41 to 48 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 42 to 48; every display formalized here was checked against the renders.

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 2.2. The corollary constructs initial Θ-data from a point of a compactly bounded subset of the λ-line and applies Theorem 1.10; that uses the theory of elliptic curves, [GenEll] (Lemma 3.5, Proposition 3.4, Lemma 3.1, Proposition 1.4), [CanLift] and [IUTchI], none of which is formalized. Every arithmetic fact about the curve is a hypothesis here; what is checked is the real arithmetic of the proof:

  • the bound on θ_A (thetaA_chain) and the choice of l in (P1) (P1_upper, P1_lower, P1_l_ge_seven, P1_bound), which is condition (C1);
  • the boundedness arguments behind (P4) and (P5) (P4_bound, P5_bound) and the chain h^{1/2}·log(l) ≤ 16·δ^{1/4}·h^{3/4} (sqrt_mul_log_l);
  • the application of Theorem 1.10 (apply_theorem_1_10), the comparison of log(𝔮^{∤2}) with log(𝔮) (q_not2_step), the constant (15δ)² (fifteen_delta_step) and the assembly of the bound (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E/5)·D + (2/5)·ε_E·(1/6)·h + C_K/2 (assembly);
  • the lower bound ε_E ≥ 5·δ·h^{-1/2} (epsE_ge) and the real-power estimate showing that ε < ε_E bounds h (h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE), with the explicit constant H_unif (h_bound_Hunif);
  • the final step (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E)·D + C_K for 0 < ε_E ≤ 1 (final_step), which is the inequality (C2) after D ≤ log-diff_X(x_E) + log-cond_D(x_E) (C2_of_final).

Notation: s = h^{1/2} with h = s² = log(𝔮^∀); L = log(2δh); D = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}); Q = log(𝔮), Q2 = log(𝔮^{∤2}).

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.thetaA_chaintheorem

theorem thetaA_chain {θ s δ L ξ : ℝ} (hδ : 2 ≤ δ) (hL : 1 ≤ L) (hξ : 0 ≤ ξ) (hs : ξ ≤ s)
  (h : θ ≤ 2 * s + δ * s + 2 * δ * s * L) :
  θ ≤ 4 * δ * s * L ∧ θ ≤ -ξ + 5 * δ * s * L

**The bound on θ_A** (page 45). If θ_A ≤ 2s + δs + 2δsL (the contributions of (S1), (S3), (S2)), δ ≥ 2, L ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ ξ ≤ s, then θ_A ≤ 4δ·s·L ≤ -ξ + 5δ·s·L.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.S1_boundtheorem

theorem S1_bound {ξ s : ℝ} (hs : ξ ≤ s) (hξ : 0 ≤ ξ)
  (hθ : ∀ x : ℝ, ξ ≤ x → Chebyshev.theta x ≤ 4 / 3 * x) : Chebyshev.theta s ≤ 2 * s

The contribution of (S1): with ξ ≤ s and θ(x) ≤ (4/3)·x for x ≥ ξ, θ(s) ≤ 2s.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_uppertheorem

theorem P1_upper {l θ ξ δ s L : ℝ} (hl : l ≤ 2 * (θ + ξ)) (hθ : θ ≤ -ξ + 5 * δ * s * L) :
  l ≤ 10 * δ * s * L

(P1), upper bound. If l ≤ 2(θ_A + ξ) (Proposition 2.1 (ii)) and θ_A ≤ -ξ + 5δ·s·L, then l ≤ 10δ·s·L.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_lowertheorem

theorem P1_lower {A : Finset ℕ} {l : ℕ} {s : ℝ} (hS1 : ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → (p : ℝ) ≤ s → p ∈ A)
  (hl : l.Prime) (hlA : l ∉ A) : s < l

(P1), lower bound. If every prime ≤ s lies in A (condition (S1)) and the prime l does not, then s < l.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_l_ge_seventheorem

theorem P1_l_ge_seven {l : ℕ} {s : ℝ} (hl : l.Prime) (hs : 5 ≤ s) (hsl : s < l) : 7 ≤ l

**l ≥ 7.** Theorem 1.10 in its final form requires l ≥ 7. The paper notes on page 22 that l ≠ 5 follows from [IUTchI] Definition 3.1 (c), since the 5-torsion points are rational over F. Independently, since 5 ≤ s < l (with s = h^{1/2}) and l is prime, l ≥ 7.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_boundtheorem

theorem P1_bound {δ s : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hs : 1 ≤ s) :
  10 * δ * s * Real.log (2 * δ * s ^ 2) ≤ 20 * δ ^ 2 * (s ^ 2) ^ 2

(P1), parenthetical bound. 10δ·s·log(2δh) ≤ 20·δ²·h² for h = s², s ≥ 1, δ ≥ 1; uses Proposition 2.1 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P4_boundtheorem

theorem P4_bound {l Q T : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hT : 0 ≤ T)
  (h : (l - 2) / 24 * Q ≤ 2 * Real.log l + T) : Q ≤ 48 * (1 + T)

(P4), boundedness. (l-2)/24·Q ≤ 2·log(l) + T with l ≥ 5 and T ≥ 0 implies Q ≤ 48·(1 + T); the paper uses 1 ≤ 2·log(l) ≤ 48·(l-2)/24.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.sqrt_mul_log_ltheorem

theorem sqrt_mul_log_l {a b l : ℝ} (ha : 1 ≤ a) (hb : 1 ≤ b) (hl0 : 0 < l)
  (hl : l ≤ 20 * (a ^ 4) ^ 2 * (b ^ 4) ^ 2) :
  b ^ 2 * Real.log l ≤ 16 * a * b ^ 3

**The chain h^{1/2}·log(l) ≤ 16·δ^{1/4}·h^{3/4}** (pages 45 and 46), written with δ = a⁴, h = b⁴ (so h^{1/2} = b²). Uses (P1) l ≤ 20δ²h², 20 ≤ 5², 5 ≤ 2⁴ and Proposition 2.1 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.sqrt_mul_log_l_stepstheorem

theorem sqrt_mul_log_l_steps {a b : ℝ} (ha : 1 ≤ a) (hb : 1 ≤ b) :
  Real.log (20 * (a ^ 4) ^ 2 * (b ^ 4) ^ 2) ≤ 2 * Real.log (5 * a ^ 4 * b ^ 4) ∧
    Real.log (5 * a ^ 4 * b ^ 4) ≤ 4 * Real.log (2 * a * b)

The paper's intermediate steps of the same chain: log(20δ²h²) ≤ 2·log(5δh) and log(5δh) ≤ 4·log(2·δ^{1/4}·h^{1/4}), with δ = a⁴, h = b⁴.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P5_boundtheorem

theorem P5_bound {b B c : ℝ} (hb : 1 ≤ b) (hB : 0 ≤ B) (h : b ^ 4 ≤ B + c * b ^ 3) :
  b ≤ B + c

(P5), boundedness. If h ≤ B + c·h^{3/4} with h ≥ 1 and B, c ≥ 0, then h is bounded; written with h = b⁴: b⁴ ≤ B + c·b³ and b ≥ 1 imply b ≤ B + c.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.apply_theorem_1_10theorem

theorem apply_theorem_1_10 {Q D d dstar l E η δ s L : ℝ} (hs : 0 < s) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hd : 0 ≤ d) (hdd : 20 * d ≤ dstar) (hdδ : dstar ≤ δ) (hsl : s ≤ l)
  (hl : l ≤ 10 * δ * s * L) (hE : E ≤ dstar * l + η)
  (h : 1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + δ / s) * D + 200 * δ ^ 2 * s * L + 20 * η

Applying Theorem 1.10 (page 46). From the final inequality of Theorem 1.10 with E ≤ d*_mod·l + η_prm (Remark 1.10.7 (i): e*_mod ≤ d*_mod), 20·d_mod ≤ d*_mod ≤ δ, s ≤ l ≤ 10δ·s·L and D ≥ 0: (1/6)·Q ≤ (1 + δ/s)·D + 200·δ²·s·L + 20·η_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.q_not2_steptheorem

theorem q_not2_step {Q Q2 s l δ h : ℝ} (hs : 0 ≤ s) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hh : 1 ≤ h)
  (h1 : 1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ 1 / 6 * (s * Real.log l))
  (h2 : s * Real.log l ≤ 2 * s * Real.log (5 * δ * h)) :
  1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ s * Real.log (2 * δ * h)

**The comparison of log(𝔮^{∤2}) with log(𝔮)** (page 47). Given (1/6)·Q2 - (1/6)·Q ≤ (1/6)·s·log(l) (from (P3)) and s·log(l) ≤ 2·s·log(5δh), with δ, h ≥ 1 and 5 ≤ 2³: (1/6)·Q2 - (1/6)·Q ≤ s·log(2δh).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.fifteen_delta_steptheorem

theorem fifteen_delta_step {δ : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) :
  200 * δ ^ 2 + 1 ≤ (15 * δ) ^ 2 ∧ (15 * δ) ^ 2 ≤ 1 / 6 * (2 / 5) * (60 * δ) ^ 2

**The constant (15δ)²** (page 47): 200·δ² + 1 ≤ (15δ)² ≤ (1/6)·(2/5)·(60δ)² for δ ≥ 1; the second inequality is the paper's "6·5 ≤ 2·4²".

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.assemblytheorem

theorem assembly {Q Q2 D s δ L η B εE : ℝ} (hs : 0 < s) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hL : 0 ≤ L)
  (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hB : 1 / 6 * s ^ 2 - 1 / 6 * Q2 ≤ B)
  (hq2 : 1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ s * L)
  (hthm : 1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + δ / s) * D + 200 * δ ^ 2 * s * L + 20 * η)
  (hεE : εE = (60 * δ) ^ 2 * s⁻¹ * L) (hδε : δ / s ≤ εE / 5) :
  1 / 6 * s ^ 2 ≤ (1 + εE / 5) * D + 2 / 5 * εE * (1 / 6 * s ^ 2) + (40 * η + 2 * B) / 2

**Assembly of the bound on (1/6)·h** (page 47). With (1/6)·h - (1/6)·Q2 ≤ B (the bounded discrepancy B_K of assertion (i)), the two preceding steps, C = 40·η + 2·B, h = s², ε_E = (60δ)²·s⁻¹·L, and δ/s ≤ ε_E/5: (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E/5)·D + (2/5)·ε_E·(1/6)·h + C/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.epsE_getheorem

theorem epsE_ge {δ s L : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hL : 1 ≤ L) (hs : 0 < s) :
  5 * δ * s⁻¹ ≤ (60 * δ) ^ 2 * s⁻¹ * L

**ε_E ≥ 5·δ·h^{-1/2}** (page 47): (60δ)²·s⁻¹·L ≥ 5δ·s⁻¹ for δ ≥ 1, L ≥ 1, s > 0.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.eps_star_estimatestheorem

theorem eps_star_estimates {εd : ℝ} (h0 : 0 < εd) (h1 : εd ≤ 1) :
  1 / (1 / 2 - εd / 16) ≤ 3 ∧ (2 + εd / 16) / (1 / 2 - εd / 16) ≤ 4 + εd ∧ 4 + εd ≤ 5

The two estimates of page 47 for ε*_d = ε_d/16: 1/(1/2 - ε*_d) = 16/(8 - ε_d) ≤ 3 and (2 + ε*_d)/(1/2 - ε*_d) = (32 + ε_d)/(8 - ε_d) ≤ 4 + ε_d ≤ 5, for 0 < ε_d ≤ 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsEtheorem

theorem h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE {h δ εd ε : ℝ} (hh : 1 ≤ h) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hεd0 : 0 < εd)
  (hεd1 : εd ≤ 1) (hε0 : 0 < ε) (hε1 : ε ≤ 1)
  (hlt : ε < (60 * δ) ^ 2 * h ^ (-(1 / 2 : ℝ)) * Real.log (2 * δ * h)) :
  h < (ε * (εd / 16)) ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * (60 * δ) ^ (4 + εd)

The real-power estimate of page 47. Let h ≥ 1, δ ≥ 1, 0 < ε_d ≤ 1, ε*_d = ε_d/16 and 0 < ε ≤ 1. If ε < ε_E = (60δ)²·h^{-1/2}·log(2δh), then h < (ε·ε*_d)^{-3}·(60δ)^{4+ε_d}. For ε = 1 this is the paper's argument for (ii); for general ε it is the argument for (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.h_bound_Huniftheorem

theorem h_bound_Hunif {d εd ε : ℝ} (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hεd0 : 0 < εd) (hεd1 : εd ≤ 1)
  (hε0 : 0 < ε) :
  (ε * (εd / 16)) ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * (60 * (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * d)) ^ (4 + εd)
    ≤ 16 ^ 3 * (60 * (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5)) ^ 5 * ε ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * εd ^ (-(3 : ℝ))
      * d ^ (4 + εd)

**The explicit constant H_unif.** With δ = 2¹²·3³·5·d and d ≥ 1, the bound of h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE is at most H_unif·ε^{-3}·ε_d^{-3}·d^{4+ε_d} with H_unif = 16³·(60·2¹²·3³·5)⁵, a constant independent of K_V, d, ε_d and ε.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.final_steptheorem

theorem final_step {H D C ε : ℝ} (hε0 : 0 < ε) (hε1 : ε ≤ 1) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hC : 0 ≤ C)
  (h : 1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε / 5) * D + 2 / 5 * ε * (1 / 6 * H) + C / 2) :
  1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * D + C

The final step (page 48). For 0 < ε ≤ 1, D, C ≥ 0 and (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε/5)·D + (2/5)·ε·(1/6)·h + C/2, one has (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε)·D + C, by (1 + ε/5)/(1 - 2ε/5) ≤ 1 + ε and 1 - 2ε/5 ≥ 1/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.C2_of_finaltheorem

theorem C2_of_final {H dF fF logdiff logcond C ε : ℝ} (hε : 0 ≤ 1 + ε)
  (hdiff : logdiff = dF) (hcond : fF ≤ logcond)
  (h : 1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * (dF + fF) + C) :
  1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * (logdiff + logcond) + C

(C2) from the final step. With log-diff_X(x_E) = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) and log(𝔣^{F_tpd}) ≤ log-cond_D(x_E) (stated in Corollary 2.2 (ii)) and 1 + ε ≥ 0, the bound on log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}) becomes the bound of (C2).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3

IUT/PaperIV/Corollary2_3.lean

IUT IV, Corollary 2.3: the bounded discrepancy logic of the proof

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Corollary 2.3, its proof, and Remark 2.3.1 (ii), pages 54 and 55 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 54 and 55. The bounded discrepancy relations are those of [GenEll], Definition 1.2 (ii), with the correction of Remark 2.3.1 (ii): α ≲ β on F means that there is a constant C with α(x) - β(x) ≤ C for all x ∈ F.

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 2.3. The proof of the corollary has two parts:

  1. By [GenEll], Theorem 2.1 ((ii) implies (i)), it suffices to prove the inequality ht_{ω_X(D)} ≲ (1 + ε)·(log-diff_X + log-cond_D) on K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d} for the λ-line X = ℙ¹, D = {0, 1, ∞} and a compactly bounded K_V as in Corollary 2.2. That reduction is an external theorem and is not formalized here.
  2. On K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d}, the inequality follows from Corollary 2.2 (i), (ii) (condition (C2)) and (iii). This file checks only the logical form of that step, as an abstract lemma about real functions on an arbitrary set S (cor2_3_on_KV): an equality of bounded discrepancy classes, a bound outside a finite exceptional set, a bound on a further function outside that set and a nonnegativity condition give an inequality of bounded discrepancy classes on all of S.

Nothing here refers to curves, divisors, points of bounded degree, heights, log-differents or log-conductors: these are arbitrary real functions on an arbitrary type, and the lemma holds for every S, including S = ∅. It says nothing about K_V unless the hypotheses are established for the actual functions, which requires Corollary 2.2 and is not done here. Corollary 2.3 itself is not stated in Lean.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDledef

def BDle (S : Set α) (f g : α → ℝ) : Prop := ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ x ∈ S, f x - g x ≤ C

f ≲ g on S ([GenEll], Definition 1.2 (ii), as corrected by IUT IV, Remark 2.3.1 (ii)): there is a constant C with f(x) - g(x) ≤ C for every x ∈ S.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDeqdef

def BDeq (S : Set α) (f g : α → ℝ) : Prop := ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ x ∈ S, |f x - g x| ≤ C

f ≈ g on S: there is a constant C with |f(x) - g(x)| ≤ C for every x ∈ S.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDeq.letheorem

theorem BDeq.le {S : Set α} {f g : α → ℝ} (h : BDeq S f g) : BDle S f g

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDle.transtheorem

theorem BDle.trans {S : Set α} {f g k : α → ℝ} (h1 : BDle S f g) (h2 : BDle S g k) :
  BDle S f k

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDle_of_finite_exceptionstheorem

theorem BDle_of_finite_exceptions {S : Set α} (Exc : Finset α) {f g : α → ℝ} (C : ℝ)
  (h : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → f x - g x ≤ C) : BDle S f g

A bound that holds outside a finite exceptional set gives an inequality of bounded discrepancy classes on the whole set.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.cor2_3_on_KVtheorem

theorem cor2_3_on_KV {S : Set α} (Exc : Finset α) {ht H Λ εE : α → ℝ} {ε CK : ℝ}
  (hi : BDeq S ht (fun x => 1 / 6 * H x))
  (hC2 : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → 1 / 6 * H x ≤ (1 + εE x) * Λ x + CK)
  (hiii : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → εE x ≤ ε)
  (hΛ : ∀ x ∈ S, 0 ≤ Λ x) :
  BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε) * Λ x)

**Abstract form of the step of the proof of Corollary 2.3 on K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d}** (page 55). This is a lemma about arbitrary real functions on an arbitrary set S; it holds for every S, so it carries information about K_V only once its hypotheses are proved for the actual functions. In the intended application S is the set of points, ht the height ht_{ω_X(D)}, H the function log(𝔮^∀), Λ the function log-diff_X + log-cond_D, and εE the invariant ε_E of Corollary 2.2 (ii). Assume: * (Corollary 2.2 (i)) ht ≈ (1/6)·H on S; * (Corollary 2.2 (ii), (C2)) (1/6)·H(x) ≤ (1 + ε_E(x))·Λ(x) + C_K for x ∈ S outside a finite set Exc; * (Corollary 2.2 (iii)) ε_E(x) ≤ ε for x ∈ S outside Exc; * Λ ≥ 0 on S (log-differents and log-conductors are degrees of effective divisors). Then ht ≲ (1 + ε)·Λ on S.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDle_mono_epstheorem

theorem BDle_mono_eps {S : Set α} {ht Λ : α → ℝ} {ε ε' : ℝ} (hεε' : ε ≤ ε')
  (hΛ : ∀ x ∈ S, 0 ≤ Λ x) (h : BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε) * Λ x)) :
  BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε') * Λ x)

Corollary 2.2 (iii) is stated for ε ≤ 1. For ε > 1 the inequality follows from the case ε = 1, since Λ ≥ 0; this monotonicity is the following statement.

IUT.PaperIV.Definition1_9

IUT/PaperIV/Definition1_9.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

IUT IV, Definition 1.9 (ii): the degree deg_E of an arithmetic divisor relative to a set

Module documentation

of valuations

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, pages 21 and 22 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 21 and 22 (with a magnified crop of the display of (ii)).

Definition 1.9 (ii) of the paper: for a nonempty set E ⊆ 𝕍(F) of valuations lying over a single valuation v_ℚ of ℚ, and an arithmetic divisor 𝔞 on F, write 𝔞_E for the portion of 𝔞 supported in E and set

deg_E(𝔞) = deg(𝔞_E) / ∑_{v ∈ E} [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}].

The paper then states that deg_{E|K}(𝔞|_K) = deg_E(𝔞) for a finite extension K of F, where E|K is the set of valuations of K over E; part (i) states the analogous invariance deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) of the normalized degree.

Interpretation of the numerator. In the render of page 22 the line under deg(𝔞_E) is the fraction bar, so the numerator is the degree deg_F of Definition 1.9 (i), not the normalized degree; this file uses degF in the numerator.

Domain. degE is defined only on PlaceSet F, the finite sets E that are nonempty and whose elements all lie over one place of ℚ (LiesOverOnePlace). Interpretation choice: the place of ℚ below a valuation is encoded by placeBelow as some p, with p the residue characteristic ringChar (𝓞 F ⧸ 𝔭), for a finite place 𝔭, and as none for an archimedean place.

What is formalized: the domain (placeBelow, LiesOverOnePlace, PlaceSet), the definition (degE), the local degree [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}] (localDeg, as e_v·f_v at finite places and as InfinitePlace.mult at infinite places), basic properties (degE_add, degE_nonneg), the identification deg_E(𝔞) = deg(𝔞_E) (normalized degree) under the hypothesis that the local degrees over E sum to [F : ℚ] (degE_eq_normalizedDeg), and its unconditional instance for the set of all archimedean valuations (degE_arc_eq_normalizedDeg).

What is not formalized: the pull-back 𝔞|_K of arithmetic divisors along a finite extension K/F, the set E|K, and both invariance statements (deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) of (i) and deg_{E|K}(𝔞|_K) = deg_E(𝔞) of (ii)); the fact that the local degrees over all valuations above one finite v_ℚ sum to [F : ℚ] is also not proved here.

Public declarations

variable (F) in

IUT.PaperIV.localDegdef

noncomputable def localDeg : Valuations F → ℕ
  | .inl v => v.asIdeal.ramificationIdx ℤ * v.asIdeal.inertiaDeg ℤ
  | .inr w => w.mult

The local degree [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}]: e_v·f_v at a finite place (ramification index times inertia degree of the prime of 𝓞 F over ℤ), and 1 or 2 at an infinite place.

IUT.PaperIV.placeBelowdef

noncomputable def placeBelow : Valuations F → Option ℕ
  | .inl v => some (ringChar (𝓞 F ⧸ v.asIdeal))
  | .inr _ => none

The place of ℚ below a valuation of F: some p for a finite place 𝔭, where p is the residue characteristic of 𝔭, and none for an archimedean place.

IUT.PaperIV.LiesOverOnePlacedef

def LiesOverOnePlace (E : Finset (Valuations F)) : Prop :=
  E.Nonempty ∧ ∃ vQ : Option ℕ, ∀ v ∈ E, placeBelow v = vQ

The domain condition of Definition 1.9 (ii): E is nonempty and all its elements lie over one place v_ℚ of ℚ.

IUT.PaperIV.PlaceSetabbrev

abbrev PlaceSet : Type _ := {E : Finset (Valuations F) // LiesOverOnePlace E}

The finite sets of valuations on which Definition 1.9 (ii) defines deg_E.

IUT.PaperIV.ADiv.restrictdef

noncomputable def ADiv.restrict (E : Finset (Valuations F)) (a : ADiv F) : ADiv F :=
  a.filter (· ∈ E)

𝔞_E, the portion of 𝔞 supported in E.

IUT.PaperIV.degEdef

noncomputable def degE (E : PlaceSet F) (a : ADiv F) : ℝ :=
  degF (ADiv.restrict E.1 a) / ∑ v ∈ E.1, (localDeg v : ℝ)

Definition 1.9 (ii), with the unnormalized degree in the numerator (see the module docstring): for E a nonempty set of valuations over one place of ℚ, deg_E(𝔞) = deg_F(𝔞_E) / ∑_{v ∈ E} [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}].

  ADiv.restrict E (a + b) = ADiv.restrict E a + ADiv.restrict E b

  degE E (a + b) = degE E a + degE E b

theorem degE_nonneg {E : PlaceSet F} {a : ADiv F} (h : a.Effective) :

IUT.PaperIV.degE_eq_normalizedDegtheorem

theorem degE_eq_normalizedDeg {E : PlaceSet F} (a : ADiv F)
  (hE : ∑ v ∈ E.1, (localDeg v : ℝ) = Module.finrank ℚ F) :
  degE E a = normalizedDeg (ADiv.restrict E.1 a)

If the local degrees over E sum to [F : ℚ], then deg_E(𝔞) is the normalized degree of 𝔞_E. The hypothesis holds when E is the set of all valuations over one v_ℚ; this file proves that only for the archimedean place (sum_localDeg_arc).

The set of all archimedean valuations, i.e. the valuations over the archimedean place of ℚ.

  ⟨arcValuations F, by

    · rw [arcValuations, Finset.map_nonempty]; exact Finset.univ_nonempty

      rw [arcValuations, Finset.mem_map] at hv
      obtain ⟨w, -, rfl⟩ := hv

The set of all archimedean valuations, as an element of PlaceSet F: it is nonempty and all its elements lie over the archimedean place of ℚ.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_2.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.ElementaryFacts

IUT IV, Proposition 1.2: the constants a_i, b_i (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.2 and its proof, and the uses of a_i, b_i in the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii), pages 10, 11, 13 and 14 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 10 to 14. The definitions of a_i, b_i were checked against the render of page 10, which shows a ceiling in a_i and a floor in b_i (both lost in the text extraction).

The paper defines, for a finite extension k_i/ℚ_p with ramification index e_i,

  • a_i = (1/e_i)·⌈e_i/(p-2)⌉ if p > 2, and a_i = 2 if p = 2;
  • b_i = ⌊log(p·e_i/(p-1))/log(p)⌋ - 1/e_i.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.2. The inclusions of (i) to (iv) concern p-adic logarithms of unit groups and tensor products of rings of integers (they rest on [Kobl], p. 81, and Proposition 1.1), which need local field theory not formalized here. This file checks every elementary statement about a_i and b_i that the proposition and its proof, and the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii), use:

  • p > 2, e_i ≤ p - 2 imply a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i (a_b_of_le), and e_i = 1, p > 2 imply a_i = 1 = -b_i (a_b_of_e_one, used in (iv));
  • a_i > 1/(p-1) and p^{b_i + 1/e_i}/e_i > 1/(p-1) (a_gt, rpow_b_gt), the inputs to (i);
  • the floor estimate used in (iii) (iii_floor);
  • a_i - 1/e_i ≤ 4/p ≤ 2/log(p) and (b_i + 1/e_i)·log(p) ≤ log(2e_i) ≤ 1 + log(e_i) (a_sub_le, b_add_le), used in Proposition 1.4 (iii);
  • a_i ≥ 1/e_i for p > 2, hence d_i + a_i ≥ 1 when d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i, and d_i + a_i ≥ 2 for p = 2 when d_i ≥ 0 (d_add_a_ge), used in Proposition 1.4 (iii).

Hypotheses: p is a natural number ≥ 2 (the primality of p is not needed) and e ≥ 1.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.adef

noncomputable def a (p e : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if p = 2 then 2 else (1 / (e : ℝ)) * ⌈(e : ℝ) / ((p : ℝ) - 2)⌉

a_i of Proposition 1.2, as a function of p and e = e_i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.bdef

noncomputable def b (p e : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ⌊Real.log ((p : ℝ) * e / ((p : ℝ) - 1)) / Real.log p⌋ - 1 / (e : ℝ)

b_i of Proposition 1.2, as a function of p and e = e_i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.b_add_invtheorem

theorem b_add_inv (p e : ℕ) :
  b p e + 1 / (e : ℝ) = ⌊Real.log ((p : ℝ) * e / ((p : ℝ) - 1)) / Real.log p⌋

b_i + 1/e_i = ⌊log_p(p·e_i/(p-1))⌋.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_b_of_letheorem

theorem a_b_of_le {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 < p) (he : 1 ≤ e) (hep : e ≤ p - 2) :
  a p e = 1 / (e : ℝ) ∧ b p e = -(1 / (e : ℝ))

If p > 2 and 1 ≤ e ≤ p - 2, then a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_b_of_e_onetheorem

theorem a_b_of_e_one {p : ℕ} (hp : 2 < p) : a p 1 = 1 ∧ b p 1 = -1

Proposition 1.2 (iv) uses: if p > 2 and e_i = 1, then a_i = 1 and b_i = -1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_gttheorem

theorem a_gt {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) : 1 / ((p : ℝ) - 1) < a p e

a_i > 1/(p-1), the first input to Proposition 1.2 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.rpow_b_gttheorem

theorem rpow_b_gt {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) :
  1 / ((p : ℝ) - 1) < (p : ℝ) ^ (b p e + 1 / (e : ℝ)) / e

p^{b_i + 1/e_i}/e_i > 1/(p-1), the second input to Proposition 1.2 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.iii_floortheorem

theorem iii_floor (y a b : ℝ) (hab : a = -b) : y - 1 ≤ (⌊y - a⌋ : ℝ) - b

The estimate in the proof of Proposition 1.2 (iii): if a = -b, then for every real y, ⌊y - a⌋ - b ≥ y - 1. With y = λ - d_I this is ⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋ - b_I ≥ λ - d_I - 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_sub_letheorem

theorem a_sub_le {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) : a p e - 1 / (e : ℝ) ≤ 4 / p

a_i - 1/e_i ≤ 4/p, used in the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.four_div_letheorem

theorem four_div_le {p : ℝ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) : 4 / p ≤ 2 / Real.log p

4/p ≤ 2/log(p) for p ≥ 2, used in the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.b_add_letheorem

theorem b_add_le {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) :
  (b p e + 1 / (e : ℝ)) * Real.log p ≤ Real.log (2 * e) ∧
    Real.log (2 * e) ≤ 1 + Real.log e

(b_i + 1/e_i)·log(p) ≤ log(2e_i) ≤ 1 + log(e_i), used in the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_ge_invtheorem

theorem a_ge_inv {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 < p) (he : 1 ≤ e) : 1 / (e : ℝ) ≤ a p e

a_i ≥ 1/e_i for p > 2.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.d_add_a_getheorem

theorem d_add_a_ge {p e : ℕ} {d : ℝ} (he : 1 ≤ e) :
  (2 < p → ((e : ℝ) - 1) / e ≤ d → 1 ≤ d + a p e) ∧ (p = 2 → 0 ≤ d → 2 ≤ d + a p e)

d_i + a_i ≥ 1 when p > 2 and d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i (Proposition 1.3 (i) with k_0 = ℚ_p); d_i + a_i ≥ 2 when p = 2 and d_i ≥ 0. Used in Proposition 1.4 (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_3.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.3: estimates of differents (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.3 and its proof, pages 11 to 13 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 11, 12 and 13; the displays formalized here were checked against the renders.

Statement of the paper (with d_i, d_0 the orders of generators of the differents of k_i, k_0 over ℚ_p, e_0 the ramification index of k_0, e_{i/0} = e_i/e_0, n_i the p-adic valuation of [k_i : k_0]):

  • (i) d_i ≥ d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/(e_{i/0}·e_0) = d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i, with equality when k_i/k_0 is tamely ramified;
  • (ii) if k_i is Galois over some k_1 ⊇ k_0 with k_1/k_0 tame, then d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.3. The proof computes differents through Eisenstein and Kummer polynomials over local rings, which needs local field theory not formalized here. This file checks the arithmetic of the proof:

  • the minimum in the display of (i) (i_min);
  • the base case n_i = 0 of the induction in (ii), i.e. that (i) with equality gives d_i ≤ d_0 + 1/e_0 (ii_base);
  • the chain of the inductive step of (ii) (ii_step), and the passage "it suffices to verify d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε for every ε > 0" (ii_of_forall_eps).
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.i_mintheorem

theorem i_min {e' e0 : ℝ} (he' : 1 ≤ e') (he0 : 1 ≤ e0) :
  min (1 / e0) ((e' - 1) / (e' * e0)) = (e' - 1) / (e' * e0)

The minimum in the proof of (i): for integers e' = e_{i/0} ≥ 1, e_0 ≥ 1, (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0) ≤ 1/e_0, so min(1/e_0, (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0)) = (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0), and this equals (e' - 1)/e_i with e_i = e'·e_0.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_basetheorem

theorem ii_base {di d0 e' e0 : ℝ} (he' : 1 ≤ e') (he0 : 1 ≤ e0)
  (h : di = d0 + (e' - 1) / (e' * e0)) : di ≤ d0 + 1 / e0

The base case of the induction in (ii): if d_i = d_0 + (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0) (tame case of (i)), then d_i ≤ d_0 + 1/e_0.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_steptheorem

theorem ii_step {di dstar d0 n e0 p o estar ordpw ε : ℝ} (hp : 1 ≤ p) (hε : 0 < ε)
  (hestar : p / ε ≤ estar)
  (h1 : di ≤ ordpw + dstar) (hord : ordpw = 1 + (p - 1) * o)
  (hind : dstar ≤ d0 + n - 1 + 1 / e0)
  (ho : o ≤ (p - 1) / (p * estar)) :
  di ≤ d0 + n + 1 / e0 + ε

The inductive step of (ii) (page 12). Inputs: * d_i ≤ ord(p·ϖ_i^{p-1}) + d_* (different of the Kummer extension); * ord(p·ϖ_i^{p-1}) = 1 + (p-1)·ord(ϖ_i); * the induction hypothesis d_* ≤ d_0 + n_i - 1 + 1/e_0; * ord(ϖ_i) ≤ (p-1)/(p·e_*) (the paper also states 0 ≤ ord(ϖ_i), which is not needed), and e_* ≥ p/ε. Conclusion: d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε, via (p-1)·ord(ϖ_i) ≤ (p-1)²/(p·e_*) ≤ p/e_* ≤ ε.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_of_forall_epstheorem

theorem ii_of_forall_eps {di X : ℝ} (h : ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → di ≤ X + ε) : di ≤ X

"It suffices to verify d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε for every positive ε."

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_4.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2

IUT IV, Proposition 1.4: nonarchimedean normalized log-volume estimates (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.4 and its proof, pages 13 and 14 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 13 and 14; the displays formalized here were checked against the renders.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.4. The log-volume μ^log of [AbsTopIII], Proposition 5.7, on compact open subsets of p-adic fields and their tensor products, is not formalized. Its properties used in the proof enter here as real-number hypotheses:

  • μ^log(p^k·M) = μ^log(M) - k·log(p) (normalization of (i));
  • μ^log(log_p(R_I^×)) = ∑_{i ∈ I} μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) + μ^log(R_I) (the paper's display);
  • μ^log(R_I) ≤ 0 and μ^log((R_I)^∼) = 0 ((i) together with Proposition 1.1).

What is checked:

  • the computation of (ii): (1/(e·f))·(log(1 - p^{-f}) - log(p^m·(p^f - 1))) = -(1/e + m/(e·f))·log(p) (ii_identity), so μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p) (ii_le);
  • the two summation chains of (iii) (iii_first, iii_second), with the per-index estimates of Proposition 1.2 (Proposition1_2.a_sub_le, Proposition1_2.b_add_le) and the fact that a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i for i ∉ I*;
  • the lower bounds d_I + a_I ≥ |I| (p > 2) and d_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| (p = 2) (dI_add_aI_ge).

In the first chain the paper writes p^{λ - d_I - a_I}; the render shows a floor, which is the source of the +1. Here the floor step is a hypothesis hfloor of the form μ ≤ (-λ + d_I + a_I + 1)·log(p) + μ^log(log_p(R_I^×)).

Public declarations
variable {ι : Type*} [DecidableEq ι]

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.ii_identitytheorem

theorem ii_identity {p e : ℝ} {f m : ℕ} (hp : 1 < p) (he : 0 < e) (hf : 1 ≤ f) :
  1 / (e * f) * (Real.log (1 - (p ^ f)⁻¹) - Real.log (p ^ m * (p ^ f - 1)))
    = -(1 / e + m / (e * f)) * Real.log p

Proposition 1.4 (ii), the computation. For p > 1, e, f > 0 and m ≥ 0: (1/(e·f))·(log(1 - p^{-f}) - log(p^m·(p^f - 1))) = -(1/e + m/(e·f))·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.ii_letheorem

theorem ii_le {p e mu : ℝ} {m f : ℕ} (hp : 1 < p) (he : 0 < e) (hf : 1 ≤ f)
  (hmu : mu = -(1 / e + m / (e * f)) * Real.log p) : mu ≤ -(1 / e) * Real.log p

Consequence used in (iii): μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.iii_firsttheorem

theorem iii_first {p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (I Istar : Finset ι) (hsub : Istar ⊆ I) (e : ι → ℕ)
  (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (hout : ∀ i ∈ I, i ∉ Istar → e i ≤ p - 2)
  {lam dI mu muM muR : ℝ} (mui : ι → ℝ)
  (hfloor : mu ≤ (-lam + dI + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i) + 1) * Real.log p + muM)
  (hsum : muM = ∑ i ∈ I, mui i + muR)
  (hmui : ∀ i ∈ I, mui i ≤ -(1 / (e i : ℝ)) * Real.log p) (hR : muR ≤ 0) :
  mu ≤ (-lam + dI + 1 + 4 * Istar.card / p) * Real.log p

Proposition 1.4 (iii), first chain. Let p ≥ 2, e : ι → ℕ with e_i ≥ 1, and I* ⊆ I such that e_i ≤ p - 2 for i ∈ I \ I*. Assume the floor step hfloor, the decomposition hsum, μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p) and μ^log(R_I) ≤ 0. Then μ^log(p^{⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋}·log_p(R_I^×)) ≤ (-λ + d_I + 1 + 4·|I*|/p)·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.a_add_b_letheorem

theorem a_add_b_le {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) :
  (a p e + b p e) * Real.log p ≤ 3 + Real.log e

Per-index estimate of (iii): (a_i + b_i)·log(p) ≤ 3 + log(e_i).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.iii_secondtheorem

theorem iii_second {p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (I Istar : Finset ι) (hsub : Istar ⊆ I) (e : ι → ℕ)
  (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (hout : ∀ i ∈ I, i ∉ Istar → e i ≤ p - 2) {lam dI mu : ℝ}
  (h : mu ≤ (-lam + dI + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i) + ∑ i ∈ I, b p (e i) + 1) * Real.log p) :
  mu ≤ (-lam + dI + 1) * Real.log p + ∑ i ∈ Istar, (3 + Real.log (e i))

Proposition 1.4 (iii), second chain. With the same I* and the hypothesis μ ≤ (-λ + d_I + a_I + b_I + 1)·log(p) (from μ^log((R_I)^∼) = 0 and the floor step): μ^log(p^{⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋ - b_I}·(R_I)^∼) ≤ (-λ + d_I + 1)·log(p) + ∑_{i ∈ I*} {3 + log(e_i)}.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.dI_add_aI_getheorem

theorem dI_add_aI_ge {p : ℕ} (I : Finset ι) (e : ι → ℕ) (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (d : ι → ℝ) :
  (2 < p → (∀ i ∈ I, ((e i : ℝ) - 1) / e i ≤ d i) →
      (I.card : ℝ) ≤ ∑ i ∈ I, d i + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i)) ∧
    (p = 2 → (∀ i ∈ I, 0 ≤ d i) →
      2 * (I.card : ℝ) ≤ ∑ i ∈ I, d i + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i))

**d_I + a_I ≥ |I| for p > 2, d_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| for p = 2.** Here d_I = ∑ d_i with d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i (Proposition 1.3 (i) with k_0 = ℚ_p) when p > 2, and d_i ≥ 0 when p = 2.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_5.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.5: archimedean metric estimates (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.5, pages 14 to 16 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered page 15; the statements of (i) and (iii) were checked against the render.

Statement (i) of the paper: the isomorphism of topological rings ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ ≅ ℂ ⊕ ℂ is compatible with the tensor product metric on the left and the direct sum metric on the right up to a factor of 2 (the metric on the right is 2 times the metric on the left; lengths differ by √2).

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.5. Tensor products of metrized real vector spaces, the primitive automorphisms of (ii), the integral structures B_I of (iii) and the inclusion of (iv) are not formalized. This file checks:

  • (i) in coordinates: writing an element of ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ as x₁₁·(1⊗1) + x_{i1}·(i⊗1) + x_{1i}·(1⊗i) + x_{ii}·(i⊗i) (an orthonormal basis for the tensor product metric), the isomorphism a⊗b ↦ (a·b, a·b̄) (crt, crt_pure) multiplies squared lengths by exactly 2 (crt_normSq);
  • the count in (iii): since dim_ℝ M_I = (2|V|)^{|I|} and each copy of ℂ has real dimension 2, the number of copies is 2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|} (count_iii).
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crtdef

noncomputable def crt (x11 xi1 x1i xii : ℝ) : ℂ × ℂ :=
  (⟨x11 - xii, xi1 + x1i⟩, ⟨x11 + xii, xi1 - x1i⟩)

The isomorphism ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ → ℂ ⊕ ℂ, a⊗b ↦ (a·b, a·b̄), in the coordinates (x₁₁, x_{i1}, x_{1i}, x_{ii}) with respect to the basis 1⊗1, i⊗1, 1⊗i, i⊗i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crt_puretheorem

theorem crt_pure (a b : ℂ) :
  crt (a.re * b.re) (a.im * b.re) (a.re * b.im) (a.im * b.im) = (a * b, a * (starRingEnd ℂ) b)

On a pure tensor a ⊗ b, whose coordinates are (a₁b₁, a₂b₁, a₁b₂, a₂b₂) for a = a₁ + a₂·i, b = b₁ + b₂·i, the map crt is (a·b, a·b̄).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crt_normSqtheorem

theorem crt_normSq (x11 xi1 x1i xii : ℝ) :
  Complex.normSq (crt x11 xi1 x1i xii).1 + Complex.normSq (crt x11 xi1 x1i xii).2
    = 2 * (x11 ^ 2 + xi1 ^ 2 + x1i ^ 2 + xii ^ 2)

Proposition 1.5 (i) in coordinates: the direct sum squared length of crt x is 2 times the tensor product squared length x₁₁² + x_{i1}² + x_{1i}² + x_{ii}².

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.count_iiitheorem

theorem count_iii (n v : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) : (2 * v) ^ n = 2 * (2 ^ (n - 1) * v ^ n)

The count of Proposition 1.5 (iii). For |I| ≥ 1, (2|V|)^{|I|} = 2·(2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|}), so a real vector space of dimension (2|V|)^{|I|} is a direct sum of 2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|} copies of ℂ.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_6.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.6: the consequence of the prime number theorem defining η_prm

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.6 and its proof, page 16 of the April 2020 manuscript. Page 16 was read from the extracted text and from the rendered page; both displays below were checked against the render.

The paper writes p_n for the n-th smallest prime (so p_1 = 2, p_2 = 3) and states:

  • there is an integer n₀ with n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for all n ≥ n₀;
  • "in particular" there is a positive real η_prm with ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 ≤ 4·η / (3·log(η)) for all real η ≥ η_prm, the sum over primes p ≤ η.

The paper derives the first display from the prime number theorem, cited as [DmMn], §3.10, and says the second "follows formally".

What is proved here, unconditionally: both displays, from Mathlib's prime number theorem Nat.isEquivalent_primeCounting (π(x) ~ x / log x). We also check the paper's step "the final portion follows formally", that is, the second display follows from the first one alone (counting_of_nth).

Conventions
  • ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 is Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊, the number of primes ≤ ⌊η⌋, which is the number of primes ≤ η.
  • The paper's p_n is Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1), since Mathlib's Nat.nth is 0-indexed.
  • We produce η_prm ≥ 2, which is stronger than the paper's "positive". Nothing later needs more than positivity: Step (viii) of the proof of Theorem 1.10 divides by log(η_prm) only in the case 1 < e*_mod·l < η_prm, where log(η_prm) > 0 already holds (page 31; see IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.StepVIII).
  • proposition1_6 states both displays together, as the proposition does.
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.pdef

noncomputable def p (n : ℕ) : ℕ := Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1)

The paper's p_n, the n-th smallest prime for n ≥ 1: p 1 = 2, p 2 = 3.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.p_onetheorem

theorem p_one : p 1 = 2

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.primeCounting_ptheorem

theorem primeCounting_p {n : ℕ} (hn : 1 ≤ n) : Nat.primeCounting (p n) = n

π(p_n) = n for n ≥ 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.exists_eta_prmtheorem

theorem exists_eta_prm :
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 2 ≤ ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

Proposition 1.6, second display (unconditional). There is a real η_prm ≥ 2 such that for every real η ≥ η_prm the number of primes p ≤ η is at most 4·η / (3·log(η)).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.exists_n0theorem

theorem exists_n0 :
  ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))

Proposition 1.6, first display (unconditional). There is an n₀ such that n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for every n ≥ n₀. We include 1 ≤ n among the conclusions' hypotheses because p_n is only defined by the paper for positive n.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.proposition1_6theorem

theorem proposition1_6 :
  (∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))) ∧
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 0 < ηprm ∧ 2 ≤ ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

Proposition 1.6 (both displays, unconditional): there is an n₀ with n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for all n ≥ n₀ (n ≥ 1), and there is a real η_prm > 0 (in fact ≥ 2) with ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 ≤ 4·η / (3·log(η)) for all real η ≥ η_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.div_log_monotoneOntheorem

theorem div_log_monotoneOn {x y : ℝ} (hx : Real.exp 1 ≤ x) (hxy : x ≤ y) :
  x / Real.log x ≤ y / Real.log y

The function x / log x is monotone on [e, ∞).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.counting_of_nththeorem

theorem counting_of_nth
  (h : ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))) :
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 0 < ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

"The final portion of Proposition 1.6 follows formally." The second display follows from the first one, with no further input from analytic number theory.
